# Sight stories: sources

The writing in `lib/sightStories.ts` keeps to facts that do not date quickly,
such as history, sizes and names. Hours, prices and booking steps belong to
the reservation rules and the Travel Advice guides. Each fact below names the
source it was checked against.

Judgments are Homeground's own and are written as advice. Examples: when to
arrive, what to skip, and how long to give a sight.

Checked on 2026-10-04.

## Forbidden City

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| The palace was finished in 1420. 24 emperors (14 Ming, 10 Qing) ruled from it until the 1912 abdication. | [Wikipedia: Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City); [Palace Museum](https://www.dpm.org.cn/) |
| It covers 720,000 m² (72 ha). Rooms: "over 9,000" is the usual official figure, and a 1973 survey counted 8,704. We write "close to 9,000". | [China Daily, 2023](https://china.chinadaily.com.cn/a/202301/28/WS63d4ff21a3102ada8b22cc9d.html); [Baidu Baike: 北京故宫](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E5%8C%97%E4%BA%AC%E6%95%85%E5%AE%AB/345415) |
| The Hall of Supreme Harmony is the largest hall in the palace and was used for enthronements and imperial weddings. Its roof carries ten beasts behind the immortal on a phoenix, the only building with ten. | [Wikipedia: Hall of Supreme Harmony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hall_of_Supreme_Harmony); [Wikipedia: roof decorations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_roof_decoration) |
| The Ningshou Palace area was rebuilt by Qianlong (1771–1776) for his retirement and now houses the Treasure Gallery. The Nine Dragon Screen (1771) is 29.4 m long and made of glazed tiles. | [Wikipedia: Nine-Dragon Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nine-Dragon_Wall); [Palace Museum](https://www.dpm.org.cn/) |
| Visitors enter at the Meridian Gate and leave by the north gate (Shenwumen) or the east gate. | `content/guides/forbidden-city-for-foreign-visitors/source-log.md` |
| Jingshan Park faces Shenwumen across the road. Its top pavilion, Wanchun Ting, is the classic viewpoint over the palace. | [Wikipedia: Jingshan Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jingshan_Park); [Beijing Bendibao](http://bj.bendibao.com/tour/2023112/353288.shtm) |
| The Treasure Gallery is ticketed separately. | `forbidden-city-for-foreign-visitors` guide ("individual galleries may require additional ticket") |
| Zijincheng: 紫 stands for the Pole Star (the emperor of heaven's seat), and 禁 for forbidden. Puyi lived in the inner court until he was evicted in 1924, and the Palace Museum opened in 1925. | [Wikipedia: Forbidden City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbidden_City) |
| Courtyards widen up to the square below the Hall of Supreme Harmony (about 30,000 m², the largest in the palace, with bronze rank markers for officials) and narrow after the Gate of Heavenly Purity (乾清门). | [Baidu Baike: 太和殿前广场](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E5%A4%AA%E5%92%8C%E6%AE%BF%E5%89%8D%E5%B9%BF%E5%9C%BA/5986850); [Baidu Baike: 品级山](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E5%93%81%E7%BA%A7%E5%B1%B1/10895622) |
| The tenth roof beast, hangshi (行什), is monkey-faced and winged and appears only on the Hall of Supreme Harmony. | [Palace Museum lemma](https://www.dpm.org.cn/lemmas/241413.html) |
| Qianlong rebuilt the Ningshou courts for his retirement but never moved in. The jade mountain "Yu Taming the Flood" (about 5,350 kg, 2.24 m tall), the largest jade carving in the palace, stands in the Hall of Joyful Longevity (乐寿堂). | [World Monuments Fund: Qianlong Garden](https://www.wmf.org/monuments/qianlong-garden); [Palace Museum: 乐寿堂](https://www.dpm.org.cn/explore/building/236470.html); [Baidu Baike: 大禹治水图玉山](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E6%B8%85%E5%A4%A7%E7%A6%B9%E6%B2%BB%E6%B0%B4%E5%9B%BE%E7%8E%89%E5%B1%B1/1548501) |
| The Treasure Gallery is ticketed separately, and the guide says to secure it before the day. | [Palace Museum notice](https://www.dpm.org.cn/singles_detail/257830.html); `forbidden-city-for-foreign-visitors` guide |
| For slower walkers, use the museum's two-hour route and don't add Jingshan automatically. | `forbidden-city-for-foreign-visitors` guide (parents callout, route cuts) |
| 65–75% of visitors enter between 08:30 and 10:30, so the entrance is busiest at opening; inside, the halls are busiest from about 10:00 to the early afternoon. Cixi lived in the Hall of Joyful Longevity from 1894, so the courts were used, though not by Qianlong. | [Beijing government, 2023](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/renwen/cshd/202310/t20231025_3286234.html); [Palace Museum: 乐寿堂](https://www.dpm.org.cn/explore/building/236470.html) |
| Korean: Gyeonghoeru has 11 japsang (the most in Korea) and Geunjeongjeon has 7. Counting the lead figure, the Hall of Supreme Harmony also has 11. | [Namu wiki: 경회루](https://namu.wiki/w/%EA%B2%BD%EB%B3%B5%EA%B6%81%20%EA%B2%BD%ED%9A%8C%EB%A3%A8); [OhmyNews](https://www.ohmynews.com/NWS_Web/View/at_pg.aspx?CNTN_CD=A0000880174) |

## The Great Wall

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Badaling is about 60 km north-west of central Beijing; it served as a barrier protecting the Ming capital and the Ming tombs; UNESCO listing 1987. | [Beijing government: Badaling](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/travellinginbeijing/attractions/202603/t20260320_4562521.html); [UNESCO 438](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/438/) |
| Badaling train timing (v2): the section guide lists Badaling as a fit for “a straightforward half- or full-day attraction” with the strongest independent rail links; Mutianyu has the longer road journey and a Huairou handoff. The story says Badaling by train can fit a long half-day and Mutianyu usually takes the whole day. | `content/guides/great-wall-section-selector-from-beijing/body.en.ts` (badaling-fit, transport-copy); [Beijing government: Badaling](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/travellinginbeijing/attractions/202603/t20260320_4562521.html) |
| Badaling pass fort built 1505 (弘治十八年). East gate inscription 居庸外镇 (1539), west gate 北门锁钥 (1582). Saying “居庸之险不在关而在八达岭”. Described as the earliest section of the Wall opened to visitors. | [Visit Beijing: 八达岭长城](https://s.visitbeijing.com.cn/attraction/101406); [Visit Beijing: 八达岭长城风景名胜区](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/47QrHmDi4bp) |
| Opening date: sources give 1953 (restored and opened as a scenic area) and 1958 (opened as the earliest Great Wall scenic area), so the story says “in the 1950s”. | [Visit Beijing](https://s.visitbeijing.com.cn/attraction/101406); [Visit Beijing: 爱我中华 修我长城](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/4HzI1VRWzOV) |
| More than 540 foreign heads of state and government have visited Badaling since 1954. | [Beijing News, 2025](https://m.bjnews.com.cn/detail/1747102109129893.html); [GMW, 2025](https://topics.gmw.cn/2025-05/13/content_38022831.htm) |
| Richard Nixon climbed Badaling during his February 1972 visit. | [Baidu Baike: 尼克松访华](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E5%B0%BC%E5%85%8B%E6%9D%BE%E8%AE%BF%E5%8D%8E/8665390); [NetEase](https://www.163.com/dy/article/FR8TIELP0543O9QE.html) |
| Hollow brick watchtowers: Tan Lun and Qi Jiguang pushed the programme; by 1572 the first phase had built 1,206 new towers in the Jizhen and Changzhen defence areas. Qi's plan: each tower to house 100 men with armour, weapons and provisions (台宿百人，铠仗糗粮具备); in practice 30–50 soldiers (“两旁主客军士三、五十名不等”) under a 百总 (v2: corrected from 35–50; 三、五十 means thirty to fifty). | [Yicai, book excerpt on 空心敌楼](https://www.yicai.com/news/100947769.html); [Miyun government: 戚继光在古北口](https://www.bjmy.gov.cn/stmy/zjmy/mygs/202502/t20250227_481811.html) |
| Start date of the tower programme: Qi took command of Jizhen in 1568 and building ran 1569–1572, so the story says “from the late 1560s”. Who built Badaling's towers (Tan Lun or Qi) is disputed, so both are named together. | [Yicai](https://www.yicai.com/news/100947769.html); [Sina: 八达岭空心敌台其实是谭纶主持修建](https://www.sina.cn/weibo/detail/5345973523186067.html) |
| (v2) Total length of all dynasties' walls, 21,196.18 km (SACH, 2012): correct, but cut from the story in round 1 as the one generic line in why[0]. | [Sina / SACH announcement, 2012](http://news.sina.com.cn/c/2012-06-05/164224540128.shtml) |
| Not visible to the naked eye from low Earth orbit; Yang Liwei said after Shenzhou 5 (October 2003) that he did not see it. | [NASA](https://www.nasa.gov/wp-content/uploads/static/history/PDFs/great_wall_less_great_from_space.pdf); [Scientific American](https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/no-you-cant-see-the-great-wall-of-china-from-space/) |
| Mutianyu: built by Xu Da in the early Ming (明初; v2: the story no longer gives 1368, which comes from the operator's marketing line, while the Huairou government page says only 明初 and other sources date the Zhengguantai to 1404) on Northern Qi (550–577) foundations; later rebuilt under Qi Jiguang; restoration from 1983, officially opened 1988; over 40 presidents, prime ministers and speakers have visited. | [Huairou government](https://www.bjhr.gov.cn/zjhr/lswh/tswh/201910/t20191010_414324.html); [Mutianyu official site](https://en.mutianyugreatwall.com/) |
| Mutianyu: double-sided battlements, densely spaced watchtowers, Zhengguan Pass; vegetation rate over 96 per cent. | [Beijing government: Mutianyu](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/travellinginbeijing/attractions/202603/t20260325_4566115.html) |
| Most Wall sections have crenellations on the outer side only, with a plain parapet (宇墙) inside; Mutianyu has them on both sides. | [Visit Beijing: 慕田峪长城的烟云过往](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/47QkIZA5E1m); [Beijing government: Mutianyu](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/travellinginbeijing/attractions/202603/t20260325_4566115.html) |
| Zhengguantai: three watchtowers on one platform, rare on the Wall. Open restored stretch about 2,250 m. | [Baidu Baike: 慕田峪长城](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E6%85%95%E7%94%B0%E5%B3%AA%E9%95%BF%E5%9F%8E/1897503); [Sohu: 慕田峪长城的故事](https://www.sohu.com/a/776668595_121678350) |
| Ming Tombs: Changping, 13 Ming emperors from Yongle on; the Sacred Way is lined with pairs of stone animals and officials; UNESCO 2003. | [Beijing government: Ming Tombs](https://english.beijing.gov.cn/travellinginbeijing/focus/202012/t20201215_2165105.html); [Wikipedia: Ming tombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_tombs) |
| Hongluo Temple: about 17.2 km from Mutianyu; its “three marvels” (红螺三绝: 御竹林、雌雄银杏、紫藤寄松) are a bamboo grove, a male and female ginkgo pair and a wisteria climbing a pine. Founded 338 (东晋咸康四年). (v2: the name 红螺三绝 and “male and female” are now in all three languages.) | [Visit Beijing route: 红螺寺—慕田峪](https://s.visitbeijing.com.cn/line/72); [Visit Beijing (Korean): 홍라사](https://www.visitbeijing.or.kr/article/47NWxkBmEwa) |
| Guide alignment: Mutianyu is not promised quiet; Badaling carries the heaviest crowd reputation; Jinshanling is the walkers' option, further (about 130 km) and a longer day; season notes (autumn colour not guaranteed, winter wind and ice). The story points at the section guide and does not restate the choice logic. | `content/guides/great-wall-section-selector-from-beijing/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md) |
| Korean: Qi Jiguang's 『기효신서』 reached Joseon during the Imjin War and became the drill manual of the 훈련도감 (founded 1593). | [우리역사넷 (National Institute of Korean History)](https://contents.history.go.kr/front/km/view.do?levelId=km_014_0060_0010_0010); [Korean Wikipedia: 기효신서](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EA%B8%B0%ED%9A%A8%EC%8B%A0%EC%84%9C) |
| Korean names: 팔달령, 무톈위, 진산링 (guide); 정관대, 양면 타구/성가퀴; 명십삼릉 (Korean Wikipedia); 홍라사 (Beijing government's Korean site; Trip.com KR also uses 홍뤄쓰). (v2) 공심적대(空心敵臺) for 空心敌楼, as in Korean Wikipedia's 척계광 article; 관성 and 신도 glossed once. | Guide body.ko.ts; [Trip.com KR](https://kr.trip.com/guide/attraction/%EC%A4%91%EA%B5%AD+%EB%A7%8C%EB%A6%AC%EC%9E%A5%EC%84%B1.html); [Korean Wikipedia: 명십삼릉](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EB%AA%85%EC%8B%AD%EC%82%BC%EB%A6%89) |

## Temple of Heaven

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Founded 1420 (永乐十八年) as 天地坛, where heaven and earth were worshipped together in the 大祀殿; renamed 天坛 in 1530, when separate worship began and the Circular Mound was built (v2: the story now dates the Mound to 1530 instead of implying 1420). The round hall was built as 大享殿 in 1545 and renamed 祈年殿 in 1751; 2.73 million m² (273 ha), about four times the Forbidden City; outer wall round on the north, square on the south; UNESCO 1998. | [Wikipedia (zh): 天坛](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%A4%A9%E5%9D%9B) |
| Winter-solstice sacrifice to heaven at the Circular Mound; prayers for the harvest at the Hall of Prayer early in the lunar year (孟春祈谷). | [Wikipedia (zh): 天坛](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%A4%A9%E5%9D%9B); [Beijing Daily: 举行祭天大典的圜丘坛](https://xinwen.bjd.com.cn/content/s64b49f69e4b0285efd6c99cc.html) |
| Last sacrifice to heaven: Yuan Shikai, 23 December 1914, ahead of his attempt to become emperor. Opened as a public park on 1 January 1918. | [Beijing government, 2018: 天坛公园开放百年](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/renwen/sy/whkb/201808/t20180828_1864312.html); [Beijing Daily: 天坛是如何开放的](https://bjrb-app.bjd.com.cn/content/s6a1753fde4b03fa51a7eeb0b.html) |
| Circular Mound: two low enclosing walls, round inside and square outside (天圆地方). | [Beijing Municipal Cultural Heritage Bureau: 圜丘坛建筑尺度研究](https://wwj.beijing.gov.cn/bjww/362760/362770/436348561/index.html); [Baidu Baike: 圜丘](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E5%9C%9C%E4%B8%98/4718123) |
| Circular Mound: built 1530, enlarged 1749; top tier ringed round the 天心石 by 9 slabs, then 18, up to 81 in the ninth ring; balustrade panels 72 + 108 + 180 = 360. Nine as the highest yang number standing for heaven. | [The Paper: 天坛圜丘坛的数字“密码”](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_9032526); [Wikipedia (zh): 天坛](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%A4%A9%E5%9D%9B) |
| Danbi Bridge: 360 m long, 29 m wide; about 1 m high at the south end and about 3 m at the north. | [Beijing Park Management Centre: 天坛丹陛桥](https://gygl.beijing.gov.cn/whgy/whgy_wsgc/201912/t20191206_885539.html) |
| Hall of Prayer: three-tier white marble base, blue glazed tiles, 28 pillars (4 dragon-well, 12 golden, 12 eave). | [Beijing Park Management Centre: 祈年殿](https://gygl.beijing.gov.cn/mlgy/mlgy_gyjg01/201912/t20191211_1048233.html) |
| Pillar symbolism: 4 for the seasons, 12 for the months, 12 for the twelve two-hour periods (时辰). | [Wikipedia (zh): 天坛](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%A4%A9%E5%9D%9B); [Sina Travel, 2008](http://travel.sina.com.cn/china/2008-08-05/134312340.shtml) |
| Roof colours: the Jiajing-era hall had blue (top), yellow and green roofs; Qianlong changed all three to blue (sources give 1751 or 1752, so the story says “the 1750s”). | [Chinanews, 2015](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/m/cul/2015/06-04/7322194.shtml); [Baidu Baike: 祈年殿](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E7%A5%88%E5%B9%B4%E6%AE%BF/2034363) |
| Lightning fire 1889; rebuilt 1890–1896 to the old form. | [Wikipedia (zh): 天坛](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%A4%A9%E5%9D%9B); [CCTV: 时光里的天坛](https://news.cctv.com/2019/12/03/ARTIqCCGrlom91Tt6mzLQGft191203.shtml) |
| Long Corridor: L-shaped (呈曲尺形; v2: now in en too), east of the Qigu altar, built 1420 with 75 bays, 72 since 1752; a closed passage for carrying sacrificial offerings between the slaughter pavilion, divine kitchen and altar; turned into an open walkway in 1937. | [Beijing Park Management Centre, 2023](https://gygl.beijing.gov.cn/xxgk/xxgk_gyxx/202307/t20230718_3164675.html); [Beijing Evening News, 2024](https://www.takefoto.cn/news/2024/01/09/90033266.shtml) |
| Card and chess players fill the corridor benches, especially on winter mornings. | [Xiao Fuxing, 天坛十三记, People's Literature 2020](http://www.chinawriter.com.cn/n1/2020/1009/c418925-31885785.html) |
| 3,562 old trees, most of them cypresses; green space 163 ha. | [Beijing Daily, 2022: 古树寻踪](https://news.bjd.com.cn/2022/07/05/10112856.shtml); [Visit Beijing: 天坛古柏](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/47c1v4kQlmK) |
| Older residents come for morning exercise and evening walks; the park added 45 ramps and some 200 coat racks by its exercise areas. | [CCTV, 2017](http://gongyi.cctv.com/2017/04/17/PHOATOajh6AM9Qr90xvcrOjJ170417.shtml) |
| The outer park opens at 06:00 (06:30 in winter); the monuments open at 08:00. The story says only “well before the monuments”. | [Beijing Park Management Centre, 2023](https://gygl.beijing.gov.cn/xxgk/xxgk_gyxx/202311/t20231115_3302035.html) |
| Line 8 runs Qianmen – Zhushikou – Tianqiao, so Qianmen is two stops north of Tianqiao. | [Wikipedia (zh): 北京地铁8号线](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E5%8C%97%E4%BA%AC%E5%9C%B0%E9%93%818%E5%8F%B7%E7%BA%BF) |
| Guide alignment: allow two to three hours; Echo Wall acoustics depend on conditions and are not guaranteed; park-only ticket covers the outer park but not the three core sights. | `content/guides/temple-of-heaven-gates-and-ritual-sequence/` (body.en.ts); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` (`temple-of-heaven`) |
| Korean: Gojong built Hwangudan (圜丘壇) in 1897 and sacrificed to heaven there on becoming emperor of the Korean Empire; Hwanggungu (皇穹宇) was added in 1899; the altar itself was demolished in 1913. Only the Chinese emperor traditionally performed the heaven sacrifice. | [Korean Wikipedia: 환구단](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%ED%99%98%EA%B5%AC%EB%8B%A8); [Munhwa Ilbo, 2018](http://www.munhwa.com/news/view.html?no=2018022101033427097001) |
| Korean: Yuan Shikai entered Joseon in 1882 with Wu Changqing's troops after the Imo Incident, was the Qing resident from October 1885, and left Seoul in July 1894, twelve years in all; he interfered in its government (v2: corrected from “1885~1894년 조선에 머물며”). | [우리역사넷](https://contents.history.go.kr/front/nh/print.do?levelId=nh_039_0020_0010_0020&whereStr=); [Wikipedia: Yuan Shikai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_Shikai); [우리역사넷: 임오군란](https://contents.history.go.kr/mobile/kc/view.do?levelId=kc_i403200) |
| Korean names: 기년전, 회음벽, 원구단, 황궁우, 단폐교 (guide body.ko.ts; Tripstore uses 원구단, the Beijing government's Korean site 환구단); 전문대가 (Trip.com KR, Hanatour, visitbeijing.or.kr). (v2) The Temple of Heaven corridor is 칠십이장랑, to tell it apart from the Summer Palace's 장랑 (wiki1.kr / 세계여행위키 「천단」: “칠십이 장랑(七十二长廊)”). 大栅栏 is written 다자란(大柵欄): Trip.com KR's 전문대가 page uses 다자란 throughout and 대책란 once; the hanja covers readers who know 대책란. Seoul's altar is 환구단 (문화재청 standard), with 원구단 as the other reading of 圜丘. | [Tripstore](https://www.tripstore.kr/blog/%EB%B2%A0%EC%9D%B4%EC%A7%95-%EC%B2%9C%EB%8B%A8%EA%B3%B5%EC%9B%90-%ED%95%84%EC%88%98-%EC%BD%94%EC%8A%A4); [visitbeijing.or.kr: 전문대가](https://www.visitbeijing.or.kr/article/47NWbWaBzvZ) |

## Summer Palace

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Lake works: begun in winter 1749 and finished early 1750; the old Wengshan lake (瓮山泊/西湖) was widened and deepened to twice its old extent and depth (“新湖之廓与深两倍于旧”; v2: the doubling is now in en, and zh says 湖面拓宽一倍、湖底挖深一倍) as a reservoir supplying the western imperial gardens; in 1750 Qianlong named it Kunming Lake, the hill Longevity Hill and the garden Qingyiyuan. | [Visit Beijing: 引流泉水入湖来](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/47QkJCCUp1n); [Beijing Daily: 昆明湖名称历史探源](https://xinwen.bjd.com.cn/content/s6a798fa0e4b03fa51a82be9c.html) |
| Built for the 60th birthday of Qianlong's mother; about three quarters of the 2.9 km² is water; burned by British and French troops in 1860; rebuilt by Cixi (1880s–1890s) and named Yiheyuan in 1888; damaged by the Eight-Nation Alliance in 1900 and restored within two years; UNESCO 1998. | [Wikipedia: Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Palace) |
| Funding: Admiralty (海军衙门) money went into the rebuild; the amount is disputed by historians. | [Jiang Ming, Chinese Academy of Social Sciences](http://jds.cssn.cn/newzxdt/202101/t20210128_5304029.shtml); [Wikipedia: Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Palace) |
| West Causeway modelled on Hangzhou's Su Causeway. | [Travel China Guide](https://www.travelchinaguide.com/attraction/beijing/summer/western-bank.htm); already logged for West Lake in `docs/homeground-sight-stories-sources.md` |
| Suzhou Street: Qianlong-era canal street in the style of a Jiangnan water town; eunuchs and palace maids played shopkeepers for the imperial family; burned 1860; rebuilt (work began 1986, reopened 1990; Wikipedia says 1988), so the story says “a modern rebuild”. | [Visit Beijing: 苏州街](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/47QoGPu9Kzf); [Baidu Baike: 苏州街](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E8%8B%8F%E5%B7%9E%E8%A1%97/414261) |
| Long Corridor: begun 1750, burned 1860, rebuilt 1888; 728 m, 273 bays; over 14,000 paintings; Guinness record 1990. | [Visit Beijing: 颐和园长廊](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/47Qs87fyYVC) |
| (v2) Corridor story paintings: more than 300 figure and story paintings; Romance of the Three Kingdoms is a major source, along with Water Margin, Journey to the West, Dream of the Red Chamber and other tales; the present paintings were redone in 1959 and 1979. The per-novel counts (about 60 and 26) were removed: the logged Beijing Daily link returns 404 and the split appears only on aggregator pages. | [Beijing Youth Daily via China.com.cn, 2020](http://travel.china.com.cn/txt/2020-11/16/content_76914713.html) |
| Hall of Jade Ripples: Guangxu's residence in the garden; after 1898, when Cixi came to the Summer Palace she confined him here. Brick walls were built inside 霞芬室 and 藕香榭, sealing the passages to the Hall of Benevolence and Longevity (仁寿殿) and to the lake shore; they survive (v2: en says “stayed when the court was here” and “passages … walled up”; zh/ko name the two passages; descriptions say “bricked-up passages”). | [Beijing Landscaping Bureau: 宜芸馆](https://yllhj.beijing.gov.cn/ztxx/lhysh/sh/202104/t20210423_2366890.shtml); [Wikipedia (zh): 玉澜堂](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E7%8E%89%E6%BE%9C%E5%A0%82) |
| Marble Boat (清晏舫): built 1755, 36 m stone hull; Chinese cabin burned 1860; rebuilt 1893 with a Western-style cabin and carved paddle wheels on the sides; the cabin is painted wood that looks like stone. | [Baidu Baike: 清晏舫](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E6%B8%85%E6%99%8F%E8%88%AB/3418439); [360 Baike: 清晏舫](https://baike.so.com/doc/3004035-3168013.html); [Wikipedia: Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Palace) |
| First Sino-Japanese War 1894–95; the Beiyang Fleet was destroyed (v2: en now says the Beiyang Fleet, not “China's navy”; the Nanyang Fleet survived). | [Wikipedia: First Sino-Japanese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Sino-Japanese_War) |
| Magnolias at the Hall of Happiness and Longevity (Cixi's residence): planted in rows before the hall under Qianlong (玉香海); all were destroyed in 1860 except one by the Yaoyue Gate, whose trunk burned and which grew new leaves; that tree is rated about 180 years old (v2: replaces “said to date from Qianlong's time”, which the age contradicts). Peach blossom on the West Causeway in spring. | [Beijing Parks: 乐寿堂古玉兰](https://www.bjgswh.com/node/104); [Visit Beijing: spring flowers](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/4CB35Mi576P) |
| “Golden light through the arches” (金光穿洞; v2: the name is now in en too): around the winter solstice the setting sun shines through all 17 arches of the Seventeen-Arch Bridge (built 1750). | [Xinhua, 2025](http://www.news.cn/photo/20251221/d471b6418c6f492fbb6292375c8510ae/c.html); [Visit Beijing](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/47QliifOmKN) |
| Line 4 runs Beigongmen – Xiyuan – Yuanmingyuan Park, so the Old Summer Palace is one stop south of Xiyuan and two from Beigongmen (v2: Beigongmen added, since the guide's palace-to-lake route ends at the North Palace Gate). | [Wikipedia (zh): 北京地铁4号线](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E5%8C%97%E4%BA%AC%E5%9C%B0%E9%93%814%E5%8F%B7%E7%BA%BF) |
| Old Summer Palace burned by British and French troops in 1860; its European-style palaces were designed by Jesuits (Castiglione, Benoist) for Qianlong. | [Wikipedia: Old Summer Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Summer_Palace) |
| Beihai Park: imperial lake garden west of Jingshan, with the White Dagoba on Jade Flower Island. | [Wikipedia: Beihai Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beihai_Park) |
| Guide alignment: the park opens early (06:00 peak, 06:30 off-season); boats are optional, one-way and can queue at weekends; route shapes run gate to gate. | `content/guides/summer-palace-gates-route-and-boat-plan/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md) |
| Korean names: 이화원, 곤명호, 만수산, 장랑, 불향각, 십칠공교, 서제, 석방, 옥란당 (하분실, 우향사), 낙수당 (CRI Korean, eastchinatrip KR); 쑤저우제, 동궁문 (guide body.ko.ts); 원명원, 북해공원. (v2) 곤명호 glossed once as 곤명호(쿤밍호): Korean Wikipedia, 나무위키 and the West Lake story use 곤명호; Trip.com KR, eastchinatrip KR and the site guide use 쿤밍호. 청안방 shown with hanja (淸晏舫): no market hit. 청의원 for 清漪园, 요월문 for 邀月门, 인수전 for 仁寿殿, 백탑 for Beihai's White Dagoba, 베이궁먼역 for Beigongmen. Book titles in 『』 (『삼국지연의』, 『서유기』, 『기효신서』). | [CRI Korean: 이화원](http://korean.cri.cn/1760/2014/08/12/1s216613.htm); [eastchinatrip KR](https://www.eastchinatrip.com/ko/summer-palace-beijing-guide-kr/) |

## National Museum of China

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Origins in 1912, when Cai Yuanpei, Lu Xun and others pushed for the National History Museum Preparatory Office; merger of the Museum of Chinese History and the Museum of the Chinese Revolution in February 2003; rebuilding completed 2012; nearly 200,000 m², 48 standard galleries; “the world's largest modern museum by single-building floor area” (the museum's own claim); over 1.43 million items. | [National Museum of China: 国博简介](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/gbgk/gbjj/) |
| The building opened in 1959 as one of the Ten Great Buildings for the tenth anniversary, on the east side of Tiananmen Square, housing both museums. | [Wikipedia (zh): 中国国家博物馆](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E5%8D%9A%E7%89%A9%E9%A6%86); [Beijing government](https://www.beijing.gov.cn/renwen/rwzyd/202011/t20201123_2143065.html) |
| Ancient China: 2,026 relics (2,521 exhibits in all) including 521 first-grade relics, in eight sections from remote antiquity to the Ming and Qing (v2: the eight sections replace the sentence that previewed the highlights). The Road of Rejuvenation covers China from 1840. | [Wikipedia (zh): 中国国家博物馆](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E4%B8%AD%E5%9B%BD%E5%9B%BD%E5%AE%B6%E5%8D%9A%E7%89%A9%E9%A6%86); [NMC: Ancient China](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/portals/0/web/zt/gudai/en/detail2.html); [NMC: 百年国博 · 古代中国](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/Portals/0/web/zt/100n/article_atc-3.html?id=15) |
| Houmuwu Ding: late Shang, 133 cm, 832.84 kg. The museum's English page: “It weighs 832.84 kg, making it the heaviest known bronze ware from ancient China.” The story says “heaviest known bronze vessel” (zh 青铜礼器, ko 청동 그릇) because a bare “heaviest bronze” is false: the Qin bronze chariots weigh 1,061 kg and 1,241 kg (v2: the superlative is now in all three descriptions and highlights). Xinhua says the largest surviving bronze vessel. Found in Anyang on 19 March 1939; reburied to keep it from Japanese troops; recovered in 1946; renamed from Simuwu to Houmuwu in 2011. | [NMC: Houmuwu Ding](https://en.chnmuseum.cn/collections_577/collection_highlights_608/archaeological_discoveries_609/202109/t20210902_251133.html); [People's Daily: bronze chariots](http://art.people.com.cn/n/2013/1010/c206244-23147624.html); [Xinhua, 2015](http://www.xinhuanet.com/politics/2015-08/14/c_1116261726.htm); [Wikipedia (zh): 后母戊鼎](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E5%90%8E%E6%AF%8D%E6%88%8A%E9%BC%8E) |
| Four-Ram Square Zun: Shang, 58.3 cm tall, 52.4 cm a side, about 34.5 kg; found spring 1938 in Ningxiang, Hunan; shattered into more than 20 pieces by a bomb in a wartime air raid; fragments recovered and restored in 1952; transferred to the Museum of Chinese History in 1959. Shown in Ancient China, section II. | [NMC: 百年国博 · 四羊方尊](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/Portals/0/web/zt/100n/guobao_content-8.html); [NMC: Ancient China II](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/portals/0/web/zt/gudai/en/detail2.html) |
| Storyteller Beating a Drum: Eastern Han, 56 cm; excavated 1957 from Tomb 3 at Tianhuishan, Chengdu; bare chest and belly, drum in the left arm, stick in the right hand, laughing; Han storytellers drummed while singing their tales. Shown in Ancient China, section IV. | [NMC: Pottery Storyteller Beating a Drum](https://en.chnmuseum.cn/collections_577/collection_highlights_608/archaeological_discoveries_609/202008/t20200831_247541.html); [NMC: Ancient China IV](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/portals/0/web/zt/gudai/en/detail4.html) |
| Summer holidays are the hardest weeks to get a reservation. | [The Paper, 2025](https://www.thepaper.cn/newsdetail_forward_31119054); [GMW, 2025](https://m.gmw.cn/2025-07/02/content_1304073355.htm) |
| Line 1 runs Tian'anmen East – Wangfujing, one stop apart. | [Beijing Subway](https://www.bjsubway.com/) (line map) |
| Guide alignment: about three hours is the balanced first visit (one anchor exhibition, a break, one compact second section); Ancient China is the recommended anchor; the museum booking does not cover Tiananmen Square, which has its own access and security. | `content/guides/national-museum-of-china-booking-and-route/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` (`national-museum-of-china`) |
| Shaanxi History Museum covers the Zhou to the Tang (matches the site's own card line). | `lib/sightsI18n.ts` (`shaanxi-history-museum`) |
| Korean names: 중국 국가박물관, 고대 중국 (guide body.ko.ts); 톈안먼 (site standard); 후모무정, 사모무정 (나무위키 「무정(상)」); 사양방존 (ithemove.com); 부흥의 길 (동북아역사재단 webzine); 설창용 is a descriptive hanja reading, glossed in the body. (v2) 후한(동한) for Eastern Han, following Korean historiography; 미세먼지 for haze. | Guide body.ko.ts; review1-zhko.md market check |

## Beijing: open points

- Badaling's opening year: 1953 or 1958 depending on the source; written as “in the 1950s”.
- Who built Badaling's hollow towers (Tan Lun or Qi Jiguang) is disputed; both are named together.
- Hall of Prayer roof change: 1751 or 1752; written as “the 1750s”.
- Seven-Star Stones (Temple of Heaven) were left out: Beijing Daily says the present stones are a park restoration and the originals are lost.
- Oregon-pine timber in the 1890s rebuild of the Hall of Prayer was left out: the park's own page says the 28 pillars are nanmu.
- Why the Houmuwu Ding stayed on the mainland in 1949 is disputed (weight, or staff reluctance); left out.
- Suzhou Street rebuild year: 1988 (Wikipedia) or 1990 (Baidu); written as “a modern rebuild”.
- Korean names: 부흥의 길, 사양방존 and 후모무정 now have market hits (review round 1); 설창용 and 청안방 have none and are glossed or shown with hanja.
- (v2) Mutianyu's build year: 1368 (operator) against 1404 for the Zhengguantai (other sources) and 明初 (Huairou government); written as “early in the Ming” in all three languages.
- (v2) Long Corridor per-novel painting counts: unsourced, removed.
- (v2) The Yaoyue Gate magnolia: about 180 years old by the park's record, so not a Qianlong-era planting; the story says only that it is the one survivor of 1860.
- (v2) The Temple of Heaven's “round hall” dates from 1545 (as 大享殿); the story ties only the Circular Mound to 1530 and describes the harvest prayer without a start date.

## Terracotta Warriors

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| March 1974: villagers of Xiyang village, Lintong, digging a well found pottery-figure fragments (with bronze arrowheads). Yuan Zhongyi led the archaeological team from July 1974 and is known as the "father of the Qin figures". | [Bmy: Pit 1](https://www.bmy.com.cn/pitone.html) ("1974年3月，临潼区西杨村的农民打井时偶然发现了一些陶俑的残片"); [Xinhua, 2024](https://www.news.cn/politics/20240909/39a50eca54654ada85168f8ddae2e8e4/c.html); [Wenhui Bao, 2019: 袁仲一](https://wenhui.whb.cn/third/baidu/201902/27/244644.html) **(revision)** |
| Excavation and restoration have "hardly stopped" since (headline: 多年来发掘修复几乎从未停止). **(revision)** | [Chinanews, 2009-06-30](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/cul/news/2009/06-30/1754390.shtml) |
| Pit 1: 230 m east–west, 62 m wide; about 6,000 figures and horses estimated. About one fifth has been excavated. **(revision: 230 m now used in why and FAQ)** | [Bmy: Pit 1](https://www.bmy.com.cn/pitone.html) ("东西长230米…约埋藏陶俑、陶马6000件"); [GMW/CCTV, 2025](https://m.gmw.cn/2025-05/02/content_1304027818.htm) ("目前已发掘出土约五分之一") |
| The figures are life-size (typically about 1.75–2 m). Visitors view them from walkways round the pits. **(revision)** | [Wikipedia: Terracotta Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terracotta_Army); `content/guides/terracotta-warriors-without-tour/body.en.ts` (viewing rails) |
| Faces, hair and beards vary (千人千面; Xinhua uses 可谓, so the story hedges: "change from one soldier to the next"); Yuan Zhongyi counted 24 kinds of beard. | [Xinhua, 2024](https://www.news.cn/politics/20240909/39a50eca54654ada85168f8ddae2e8e4/c.html); [Cnwest, 2025](http://m.cnwest.com/sxxw/a/2025/02/26/23009366.html); [Xinhua Shaanxi, 2025](http://www.sn.xinhuanet.com/20250226/5b1e9615bef04c28abc82b480cfd9845/c.html) |
| Potters' names stamped or carved in inconspicuous places; the 物勒工名 practice: makers marked their work so faults could be traced. (The 宫/咸阳 prefixes were checked but are no longer used in the story.) | [Cnwest, 2025](http://m.cnwest.com/sxxw/a/2025/02/26/23009366.html); [Chinanews: Tian Jing interview, 2021](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/gn/2021/12-13/9628672.shtml) |
| Figures were painted over a base coat of raw lacquer, in more than a dozen colours. Surviving paint starts to change about 15 seconds after excavation and can completely dehydrate, curl and flake within four minutes (Xia Yin). The story says "starts to change" (not "curl") at 15 seconds, per review finding 5. | [Xinhua, 2017](http://www.xinhuanet.com/politics/2017-09/16/c_1121673179.htm); [Xinhua, 2024](https://www.news.cn/politics/20240909/39a50eca54654ada85168f8ddae2e8e4/c.html) ("那些保存下来的颜料出土后15秒就开始变化，4分钟内就完全脱水、起翘、剥落") |
| Pit 1 was burned and then soaked, so its figures lost almost all colour. | [China Story / People's Political Consultative News](https://www.chinastory.cn/PCzwdbk/detail_v2/20190807/1006000000039761565145609138258368_1.html); [The Paper, 2023](https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_24871042) ("陶俑埋在地下两千多年，经过火烧水淹…") **(revision)** |
| Excavators work slowly and carefully because of the paint: after the 1974 colour loss, later digs were made "particularly meticulous" to protect the figures. The story says "So the excavators work slowly", not that paint is the only reason. **(revision)** | [Chinanews, 2009-06-22](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/cul/news/2009/06-22/1742902.shtml) ("其间要特别细致一些…还要考虑陶俑自身的保护问题") |
| The pits lie about 1.5 km east of the tomb mound, outside the mausoleum's outer wall ("outer edge" of the tomb). The mound is largely unexcavated and the chamber has never been opened. Sima Qian (Shiji): 以水银为百川江河大海; anomalously high mercury in the soil of the mound. **(revision: outer-wall position)** | [Wikipedia: Mausoleum of Qin Shi Huang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mausoleum_of_Qin_Shi_Huang); [People's Daily (Sichuan), 2021](http://sc.people.com.cn/n2/2021/0510/c345167-34716168.html) |
| Lishan Garden is the site park round the mausoleum and holds the Bronze Chariots Museum; one ticket covers the pits and Lishan Garden, with an included shuttle. | `content/guides/terracotta-warriors-without-tour/body.en.ts` (one-ticket, lishan-contents); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` (`terracotta-warriors`) |
| "Pit 1 gives you the scale, Pit 2 the close-up detail" follows the guide's pit table (Pit 1 for scale and formation; Pit 2 for troop types and closer display objects). | `content/guides/terracotta-warriors-without-tour/body.en.ts` (pits-table) |
| The army faces east, so in Pit 1 the east end is the front. The front area is robe-wearing figures without armour, the light-infantry vanguard. | [Wikipedia: Terracotta Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terracotta_Army); [The Paper, 2023 (Shen Maosheng)](https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_22322221); [People's Pictorial, 2024](http://www.rmhb.com.cn/wh/202405/t20240522_800366836.html) |
| A restoration area sits at the back (west side) of Pit 1, on the pit floor, where restorers can be seen working. The 2017 source adds "every morning"; that timing is not used (old source, would be a timing promise). | [China Writer, 2017](https://www.chinawriter.com.cn/n1/2017/0913/c404008-29533468.html) ("在一号坑展厅后部，坑底有一小片修复工作区"); [tang.org.cn, 2025-12](https://www.tang.org.cn/78314.html) ("西侧修复区可观看工作人员现场修复") **(revision)** |
| Pit 2: mixed archers, chariots, cavalry and infantry. The display reopened (trial) on 1 October 2026. Photos of 30 September show single figures including a cavalryman with saddled horse, a standing archer and a mid-ranking officer (中级军吏俑); Wen Wei Po reports a display case showing the figures' original colours (彩色秦俑科普展示櫃). The story no longer says "glass cases" or points at the green-faced archer. **(revision)** | [Bmy: Pit 2](https://www.bmy.com.cn/pittwo.html); [Xinhua photos, 2026-09-30](https://www.news.cn/photo/20260930/1ef4763c57374354a34e6488ce379240/c.html); [Wen Wei Po, 2026-10-01](https://www.wenweipo.com/a/202610/01/AP6abe1db4e4b01d54a28585a7.html) |
| Bronze chariots: found December 1980, 20 m west of the mound ("beside the mound"), crushed; half life-size; each drawn by four horses; more than 3,000 fragments; restoration took 8 years; gold and silver fittings 14 kg on the pair. Both are now shown together in the Bronze Chariots Museum in Lishan Garden ("同框"; the story says "shown together", not "side by side"). | [Bmy: 铜车马坑](https://www.bmy.com.cn/pithorse.html); [Chinanews, 2024](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/cul/2024/04-18/10201202.shtml); [Cnwest, 2024](http://m.cnwest.com/sxxw/a/2024/04/19/22529642.html) |
| Holiday sell-out: during National Day 2026 the daily allocation sold out on 2–4 October. Story and FAQ say only "the day's tickets can sell out". | [Sina / Daqing Evening News, 2026](https://sina.cn/weibo/detail/5349962670148236.html); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` ("Sales can stop at capacity") |
| Huaqing Palace: by Huaqingchi station on Metro Line 9; Tang Xuanzong and Yang Guifei; Bai Juyi's 长恨歌 line 春寒赐浴华清池; site of the 1936 Xi'an Incident, when Chiang Kai-shek was seized by his own generals (Zhang Xueliang and Yang Hucheng; names left out in all three languages). **(revision: poem line, Korean names)** | [Xi'an Bendibao: 华清宫交通](https://m.xa.bendibao.com/tour/120801.shtm); [Wikipedia: Huaqing Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huaqing_Palace); [Wikisource: 長恨歌](https://zh.wikisource.org/wiki/%E9%95%B7%E6%81%A8%E6%AD%8C) ("春寒賜浴華清池，溫泉水滑洗凝脂"); [Korean Wikipedia: 시안 사건](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EC%8B%9C%EC%95%88_%EC%82%AC%EA%B1%B4) (title 시안 사건; place given as 화청지) |

Not used, or unsettled:
- Total figures in the three pits: Xinhua 2024 and CCTV say about 8,000; Wen Wei Po 2026 says over 7,000; the museum's own pit figures add up to about 7,400. Only Pit 1's 6,000 is used.
- The Xu Fu / Seogwipo legend (ko only) was cut in the revision: true as a legend, but nothing on site connects to it (review findings 24 and zhko 13).
- The 1999 green-faced kneeling archer and the kneeling archers' sideways fall: dropped, because the current Pit 2 display could not be confirmed to show them.
- "More than 600 pits and tombs", the 221/210 BC dates and the 宫/咸阳 stamp prefixes: true, but cut as counts and dates with no use on site.
- Mound height: sources give 35.5 m to 87 m. Not used.

## Xi'an City Wall

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Circuit 13.74 km; 12 m high; 12–14 m wide on top. 98 platforms (敌台), each projecting 12 m, 120 m apart, within range of bows and firearms. | [Qujiang government, 2026](https://qjxq.xa.gov.cn/xwzx/xwdt/2032394805232328706.html) (13.74 km); [Wikipedia (zh): 西安城墙](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A5%BF%E5%AE%89%E5%9F%8E%E5%A2%99) ("城顶宽12-14米"; "各自相距120米，恰在弓箭和火铳的射程范围内") |
| A ring park runs along the moat round the wall. Cycling on the wall is an official visitor activity. **(revision)** | [Wikipedia (zh): 西安城墙](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A5%BF%E5%AE%89%E5%9F%8E%E5%A2%99) ("环绕城墙设立了环城公园"); [Qujiang government reply, 2026-07-20](https://qjxq.xa.gov.cn/zwgk/ndzdxxgk/jytabl/2079470382543396866.html) |
| At the end of the Tang, Chang'an was burned repeatedly; in 904 the governor (Han Jian; not named in the story) gave up the outer city and palace city and kept only the imperial city. | [Wikipedia (zh): 西安城墙](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A5%BF%E5%AE%89%E5%9F%8E%E5%A2%99); [Cnwest, 2021](http://m.cnwest.com/cyzx/a/2021/09/22/19973459.html) |
| The Ming wall was finished in 1378; west and south follow the Tang imperial-city line; east and north were extended. | [Chinanews, 2009](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/cul/news/2009/10-29/1938145.shtml); [Cnwest, 2021](http://m.cnwest.com/cyzx/a/2021/09/22/19973459.html); [Wikipedia: Fortifications of Xi'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortifications_of_Xi%27an) |
| The Ming sealed Hanguang Gate's last open passage when they rebuilt the wall, and the gate survived because it was wrapped inside the wall ("也正因为含光门被包在城墙之内，其遗址才能保存至今"). **(revision)** | [Cnwest / Xi'an Evening News, 2024-08-14](http://m.cnwest.com/cyzx/a/2024/08/14/22736183.html) |
| The walled city is about 12 km² (Chinanews: 11.9 km²), about one seventh of Tang Chang'an. English Wikipedia says "about an eighth"; the story keeps "about a seventh" with the Chinese sources and does not give km². | [Wikipedia (zh): 西安城墙](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A5%BF%E5%AE%89%E5%9F%8E%E5%A2%99); [Chinanews, 2009](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/cul/news/2009/10-29/1938145.shtml); [Wikipedia: Chang'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang%27an) |
| 1958: as Beijing pulled down its wall, Xi'an decided to demolish; bricks on the battlements were almost all stripped (城墙垛口的砖几乎被拆尽) and the western stretch of the south wall lost its brick facing. Five heritage workers sent a telegram to the State Council in the name of the Shaanxi Cultural Relics Committee; the State Council notice to protect the wall was issued on 22 July 1959; first-batch national protection 1961 (date no longer used). **(revision: telegram wording)** | [Chinanews, 2015](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/m/gn/2015/03-06/7108190.shtml); [Cnwest, 2021](http://m.cnwest.com/cyzx/a/2021/09/22/19973459.html) |
| Major repair programme from 1983; almost all stretches restored or rebuilt. The full ring was reconnected at the railway station: zh Wikipedia says 26 December 2004, en Wikipedia says May 2005, so the story says "the mid-2000s" (zh: 2005 年前后). | [Cnwest, 2021](http://m.cnwest.com/cyzx/a/2021/09/22/19973459.html); [Wikipedia (zh)](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A5%BF%E5%AE%89%E5%9F%8E%E5%A2%99) ("直到2004年12月26日，火车站前城墙才完成合拢"); [Wikipedia: Fortifications of Xi'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortifications_of_Xi%27an) |
| The four main gates were each built three towers deep (闸楼 working the drawbridge, 箭楼 with arrow windows, 正楼) around an enclosed courtyard that trapped attackers. All four 闸楼 were pulled down in the early Republic; only Yongning (South) Gate's was rebuilt, in September 1990, with its drawbridge. Yongning is now the one main gate with all three towers ("今永宁门完整保留了明代'门三重楼三重'的形制"). The South Gate arrow tower was destroyed in 1926 and rebuilt 2013–2014. **(revision)** | [Wikipedia (zh): 永宁门](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%B0%B8%E5%AE%81%E9%97%A8); [Wikipedia (zh): 西安城门](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A5%BF%E5%AE%89%E5%9F%8E%E9%97%A8) (the other three 闸楼 "现已无存"); [Wikipedia (zh): 西安城墙](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A5%BF%E5%AE%89%E5%9F%8E%E5%A2%99) ("民国初年，城墙的四座闸楼及其墙段被拆除") |
| Hanguang Gate: westernmost of the south wall's gates; Sui–Tang imperial-city gate first built in 582; three passages, the central one for the emperor; clear Tang cart ruts in the west passage; the museum protects the gate passages, a wall cross-section (城墙断面) and a culvert; museum opened 27 September 2008. What survives is the gate's passages and walls, not the whole gate (the middle–west partition stands only about 1 m), so the story says "what is left of". **(revision)** | [Cnwest / Xi'an Evening News, 2024-08-14](http://m.cnwest.com/cyzx/a/2024/08/14/22736183.html); [Chinanews, 2008](https://www.chinanews.com/cul/news/2008/09-27/1396235.shtml) |
| The southwest corner is the only round one of the four. Explanations differ: Tencent cites the Sui–Tang imperial-city corner; zh Wikipedia says it follows a Yuan-dynasty structure. The story says "the rounded corner of an older wall on the same spot". The moat curves with it. **(revision)** | [Tencent News, 2026](https://news.qq.com/rain/a/20260206A06Z5M00); [Wikipedia (zh)](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A5%BF%E5%AE%89%E5%9F%8E%E5%A2%99) ("西南隅沿袭了元代建筑为圆形") |
| Spring Festival lanterns on the wall for weeks: 2025 display 22 January–9 March (47 days); 2026 display 1 January–22 March. Story: "for weeks, in recent years into March"; FAQ: "in recent years from January into March". The 1984 founding date was cut. **(revision)** | [Cnwest, 2025-01-15](http://www.cnwest.com/tszx/a/2025/01/15/22950947.html); [Xi'an Bendibao, 2026](https://m.xa.bendibao.com/jieri/136916.shtm) |
| South Gate to the Bell Tower about 1.1 km, about 15 minutes on foot; the Muslim Quarter starts behind the Drum Tower; Shuyuanmen runs east from inside the South Gate to near the Forest of Stone Steles Museum. | [Xi'an Bendibao: 书院门](http://xa.bendibao.com/jingdian/shuyuanmen/content/); [Xi'an Bendibao: 永宁门交通](https://m.xa.bendibao.com/xiuxian/114784.shtm); [Trip.com KR: 회족거리](https://kr.trip.com/moments/detail/xi-an-7-119464584/) |
| Guide alignment: a short walk is a flexible 60–90 minute block (story now matches); a full circuit is a separate activity with no verified lap time; 17 official access points; South Gate is the easy first-visit anchor; check the exact gate's hours for an evening visit; bike rental terms unverified; event days can have their own products. Reservation rules: walk-up windows sell tickets, foreign visitors buy at staffed windows and get a paper pass, advance booking optional, online orders are exchanged for a paper pass at a window. | `content/guides/xian-city-wall-tickets-gates-walk-or-bike/body.en.ts` and `source-log.md`; [Qujiang government, 2025: 17 access points](https://qjxq.xa.gov.cn/xwzx/xwdt/1949774484239577090.html); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` (`xian-city-wall`) |
| Korean names: 시안 성벽, 남문(영녕문), 함광문, 종루, 고루, 회족거리(회민가), 서원문, 비림박물관; 남대가 not attested in the Korean market, so given with hanja 南大街. | [Trip.com KR: 시안 성벽](https://kr.trip.com/guide/attraction/%EC%8B%9C%EC%95%88+%EC%84%B1%EB%B2%BD.html); [Trip.com KR moments](https://kr.trip.com/moments/detail/xi-an-7-148884926/); review1-zhko evidence table |
| Korean hooks: projecting platforms called 치(雉), as at Suwon Hwaseong; 옹성 at Seoul's Heunginjimun (Dongdaemun) and Hwaseong's Paldalmun work on the same principle (the story says "같은 원리", not the same shape); Shuyuanmen compared with Insadong for its brush, ink and calligraphy shops. 조교 was dropped because in Korean it means a suspension bridge. | [Suwon Cultural Foundation: 팔달문](https://swcf.or.kr/?idx=44&listGubun=list&p=59&page=1&viewMode=view); [Korean Wikipedia: 수원 화성](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EC%88%98%EC%9B%90_%ED%99%94%EC%84%B1) |
| Meta: Wikidata Q1334336 = Fortifications of Xi'an (zh 西安城墙, ko 시안성벽), checked via the Wikidata API. Not a World Heritage Site, so no UNESCO URL. The operator's site (chinaxiancitywall.com) works only over HTTP, so it is not in the meta sources. **(revision)** | [Wikidata Q1334336](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q1334336) |

Not used, or unsettled:
- "China's most complete surviving city wall" is the operator's claim; not used.
- Whether the Hanguang Gate museum is covered by the wall ticket (Bendibao says yes) is a ticketing fact for the guide; not stated.
- Exact distance from Hanguang Gate to the southwest corner was not found; the story gives only the direction.
- The envoys' office (鸿胪寺) just inside Hanguang Gate (review finding 25, optional): declined, because nothing of it is visible.
- Lantern-zone ticket prices in 2025 differed from the standard fare; prices belong to the guide.

## Shaanxi History Museum

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Around Xi'an, emperors lie under man-made mounds (Qin, Han) and natural mountains (14 of the 18 Tang emperors used mountains as their tomb mounds). Tang Chang'an lies under the modern city (the Ming walled city sits on its imperial quarter; Hanguang Gate survives inside the wall). **(revision)** | [Wikipedia: Qianling Mausoleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qianling_Mausoleum) ("Of the 18 emperors of the Tang dynasty, 14 of these had natural mountains serving as the earthen mounds for their tombs"); [Wikipedia: Mausoleum of Qin Shi Huang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mausoleum_of_Qin_Shi_Huang); [Wikipedia: Chang'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chang%27an) |
| Shaanxi held the capitals of the Zhou, Qin, Han and Tang; the basic exhibition stresses those four. Basic exhibition 陕西古代文明: over 3,000 objects, three halls, seven stages, from humans 1.63 million years ago to before the 1840 Opium War. (The 2018 rework date and the 1.7 million collection count were cut.) | [SHM: 常设展览](https://www.sxhm.com/basic_display.html) ("用三千余件文物来表现陕西从163万年前古人类出现一直到1840年鸦片战争以前"; "着重表现周、秦、汉、唐"); [SHM: 关于](https://www.sxhm.com/about.html) |
| Murals: nearly 600 from over 20 Tang tombs, over 1,000 m² (1000多平方米; the draft's "about" was corrected); the gallery is in the basement of the east exhibition building; the museum's highlights include 客使图, 马球图, 宫女图, 狩猎出行图 and 乐舞图 ("hunting parties setting out, palace women, musicians and dancers"). **(revision)** | [SHM: 唐代壁画珍品馆](https://www.sxhm.com/tang_mural.html) ("共有20多座唐墓的壁画精品近600幅，达1000多平方米"; "东展厅地下一层") |
| The murals come from tombs including those of Li Shou, Crown Prince Yide, Prince Zhanghuai and Princess Yongtai ("princes and a princess"); painted on earthen walls, they fade and flake; before the gallery they were kept in store; the gallery opened 20 June 2011 (Italian cooperation, date and partner no longer in the story); the cases hold constant temperature and humidity and are sealed. **(revision)** | [SHM news (Chinanews), 2011](https://www.sxhm.com/info/industry/detail/15161.html) ("李寿墓、懿德太子墓、章怀太子墓、永泰公主墓等14座墓葬"; "壁画大多绘于土壁之上…脱色、掉渣"; "恒温恒湿…密封性好") |
| Two venues: the Main Building in central Xi'an (91 Xiaozhai East Road) and the Qin-Han Gallery in Xixian New Area (opened May 2024), with separate entry rules. Basic admission to the Main Building is free with a real-name reservation; the Tang mural gallery is a separate paid ticket (sold online in advance and on site). | `content/guides/shaanxi-history-museum-booking-and-collection-plan/body.en.ts` (venue-warning, paid-exhibitions); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` (`shaanxi-history-museum`); [SHM: 导览](https://www.sxhm.com/guide.h) |
| Architect Zhang Jinqiu designed it as a highly abstracted Tang palace (central hall, a tower at each corner) in black, white and grey instead of imperial red walls and yellow tiles. (Her pupil-of-Liang-Sicheng credential, the criticism at the time and her later works were cut; the draft's "went on to" chronology error goes with them.) | [Tsinghua alumni: 张锦秋](https://www.tsinghua.org.cn/info/1014/8836.htm); [SHM: 关于](https://www.sxhm.com/about.html) (中央殿堂、四隅崇楼) |
| Wusi Wei ding (五祀卫鼎), Hall 1, in the permanent exhibition: 207 characters in 19 lines cast inside, recording a land exchange in the first month of the fifth year of King Gong of Western Zhou between Qiu Wei and a neighbouring lord, approved by court ministers with an official survey; "判断西周中期青铜器的标准器"; plain form (简单大方). Still on show in Hall 1 in 2025. King Gong reigned c. 922–900 BC, hence "nearly 3,000 years ago". **(revision)** | [SHM collection 511](https://www.sxhm.com/collections/detail/511.html); [Xi'an Bendibao, 2025](https://m.xa.bendibao.com/tour/117731.shtm); [Wikipedia: King Gong of Zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Gong_of_Zhou) |
| Tang three-colour camel carrying musicians, Hall 3: seven male musicians in Han dress, seated cross-legged facing outwards and holding instruments of the Hu peoples, with a standing woman singing in the middle. **(revision)** | [SHM collection 509](https://www.sxhm.com/collections/detail/509.html) ("七名男乐手身着汉服，手持胡人所使的不同乐器…中间有一站立的女子正在歌唱") |
| Five Dynasties Yaozhou green-glazed ewer (青釉提梁倒灌壶), Hall 3: a flower calyx stands in for the lid; at the join of lid and body a nursing lioness and cub, the lioness's open mouth forming the spout; a plum-blossom filling hole in the base; works as an inverted-filling pot. **(revision)** | [SHM collection 508](https://www.sxhm.com/collections/detail/508.html) ("堆塑哺乳母子狮，母狮张口为流"; "底部中心有梅花形注水孔"); [Beijing Daily: 文博日历](https://xinwen.bjd.com.cn/content/s6653ca79e4b035c6ca5e0270.html) (does not leak upright) |
| Reception of Envoys (客使图), Prince Zhanghuai's tomb: three Tang officials and three foreign envoys; the middle envoy wears a cap with two feathers; most often identified as from Silla, with a Goguryeo view also argued. Polo mural (马球图), same tomb: 6.75 m long, twenty-odd riders. | [SHM / Sanqin Daily](https://www.sxhm.com/info/industry/detail/14666.html); [National Museum of China research paper](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/yj/xscg/xslw/201812/t20181224_36674.shtml); [SHM collection 507](https://www.sxhm.com/collections/detail/507.html); [Artron/Collection magazine, 2024](https://m-news.artron.net/news/20240413/n773956.html) |
| The summer holidays and the Spring Festival are peak booking periods; daily free places are a small fraction of demand. Story: "the hardest dates to book". **(revision)** | [Sina Travel, 2026-07-11](https://travel.sina.cn/2026-07-11/detail-inihmzut8206657.d.html?vt=4) ("特别是暑假、春节等旺季") |
| Giant Wild Goose Pagoda: built 652 to store scriptures Xuanzang brought from India; about 2 km, about 27 minutes on foot. | [Wikipedia: Giant Wild Goose Pagoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giant_Wild_Goose_Pagoda); [Xi'an Bendibao](http://m.xa.bendibao.com/tour/62047.shtm) |
| Guide alignment: 90 minutes for the broad chronology, 2–3 hours for slow reading, paid exhibitions separate; reservations open five days ahead (the story says only "a few days ahead"); a prefilled form is not a booking; connect Qin and Han to the mausoleum visit; do not squeeze the Warriors and the museum into one unbuffered morning. | `content/guides/shaanxi-history-museum-booking-and-collection-plan/body.en.ts` (answer-first, booking, route, collection-method, warriors-boundary) |
| Korean names: 산시역사박물관, 대안탑, 본관, 진한관, 시셴신구, 당대 벽화관 (guide body.ko.ts and reservation rules); 장회태자 묘 ‘예빈도’, 조우관; 당삼채; 청자 for the green-glazed ewer. | `content/guides/shaanxi-history-museum-booking-and-collection-plan/body.ko.ts`; [OhmyNews](https://www.ohmynews.com/NWS_Web/View/at_pg.aspx?CNTN_CD=A0000366339) |
| Korean hook: 격구 (polo) came through Tang China and was played in Goryeo and early Joseon. Story says only "the same family of game". | [Korean Wikipedia: 격구](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EA%B2%A9%EA%B5%AC); [Sillok wiki: 격구](https://dh.aks.ac.kr/sillokwiki/index.php/%EA%B2%A9%EA%B5%AC(%E6%93%8A%E6%AF%AC)) |
| Meta: Wikidata Q1151210 = Shaanxi History Museum (ko 산시 역사박물관), checked via the Wikidata API. Not a World Heritage Site. **(revision)** | [Wikidata Q1151210](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q1151210) |

Not used, or unsettled:
- The Hejiacun hoard is in Hall 4's paid exhibition, reported closed for renovation in June 2026; left out.
- The Du tiger tally, the Empress's jade seal and the gilt bronze silkworm moved to the Qin-Han Gallery in May 2024; left out.
- The camel and the ewer are placed in Hall 3 by the museum's own collection pages; their current display was not checked in person.
- Which murals are on show at any moment: the gallery displays a selection. The museum's own page lists the envoys and polo murals as highlights, so the story points at them; it does not promise any other mural.

## The Bund

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| The Bund runs about 1.5 km along the Huangpu, from Yan'an East Road in the south to Waibaidu Bridge over Suzhou Creek in the north. Wikipedia (en) gives "one mile (1.6 km)"; the story uses 1.5 km. | [zh Wikipedia: 外滩](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E5%A4%96%E6%BB%A9); [Wikipedia: The Bund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bund) |
| The riverside walk sits on a flood wall built in the 1990s, raised above street level (hence "raised walk"). No height is given in the story because sources disagree. | [Wikipedia: The Bund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bund) |
| The buildings are former banks, trading houses, hotels and clubs of the foreign settlement era, mostly late 19th to early 20th century; the story says "about a century ago". Domes (HSBC), columns and the Customs House clock tower. *R1:* the Bund lay in the International Settlement, run by a council elected by foreign residents, hence "when foreigners ran this part of Shanghai" (not "foreign powers"); zh 俱乐部 replaces the dated 总会; ko 조계. | [Wikipedia: The Bund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bund); [Shanghai government: Bund terraces (2024)](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-Latest-WhatsNew/20240929/ae5f74df6e4542b0b6f43b7930a98aaf.html); [Wikipedia: Shanghai International Settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_International_Settlement) |
| Customs House clock: Westminster chimes from 1928; "The East Is Red" from 1966; Westminster again from 1986; only the hourly bell 1997–2003; "The East Is Red" again since 2003, played electronically over loudspeakers (a phrase each quarter, the whole tune on the hour). | [The Paper, 2017](https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_1918168); [zh Wikipedia: 江海关大楼](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E6%B1%9F%E6%B5%B7%E5%85%B3%E5%A4%A7%E6%A5%BC) |
| Lights: the Bund, North Bund and small Lujiazui are switched on and off by the city on a published schedule (in summer 2024, on in the early evening and off late evening, fixed time, not sunset); holiday mode can extend the closing (National Day 2026: one hour later). The story gives no clock times. | [Shanghai Landscaping and City Appearance Bureau, 2024](https://lhsr.sh.gov.cn/gggs/20240429/f77bff8b7a1843d1a360eec8133fab44.html); [Sina, 2026 National Day](https://www.sina.cn/weibo/detail/5348604095235206.html) |
| *R1:* Modes: "星期一至星期四：常态模式；星期五至星期日、法定节假日：节假日模式". Holiday mode "可以运用彩色光及舒缓的动态光" (May Day 2023); 2025 May Day's major-event mode likewise used colour and gentle movement. Hence "From Friday to Sunday and on public holidays, the lights may add colour and slow movement" (hedged "may"; the 2024 notice is the latest one found that states the weekday split). | [Shanghai Landscaping and City Appearance Bureau, 2024](https://lhsr.sh.gov.cn/gggs/20240429/f77bff8b7a1843d1a360eec8133fab44.html); [CNR, 2023-04-26](https://www.cnr.cn/shanghai/gstjshanghai/20230426/t20230426_526231635.shtml); [The Paper, 2025 May Day](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_30735867) |
| Before the 18 April 1990 announcement opening Pudong for development, Lujiazui was "简陋住房组成的棚户区" with crowded ferry landings and narrow lanes. The oldest of today's towers (Oriental Pearl, 1994) is a little over thirty years old, hence "little more than thirty". | [The Paper, 2015](https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_1321191) |
| *R1:* No. 12, the former HSBC building (now SPDB): the octagonal hall's dome mosaics show the twelve zodiac signs ("十二个星座") aimed at eight large murals below of the eight cities "汇丰银行当时在全球设有分行" (Shanghai, Hong Kong, Tokyo, London, New York, Bangkok, Paris, Calcutta). Covered from the 1956 refit ("厚厚的涂饰层"); rediscovered after SPDB took the building in 1997, when workers stripped the coating, hence "uncovered only in the late 1990s". The "33 panels" count is dropped (no use on site). | [The Paper (hosted account)](https://m.thepaper.cn/baijiahao_17061083) |
| *R1:* HSBC visits in 2025: "免费，讲解须预约"; sign-up via SPDB's own channels; "每天两场……每场半小时". The story says "In 2025 the free visits ran as two half-hour guided sessions a day, booked ahead through the bank" and does not say weekdays only (the source's "工作日期间" is ambiguous) or give the clock times. The earlier "only a few places a day" had no source and is gone. | [People's Daily Shanghai, citing the Shanghai Municipal Administration of Culture and Tourism, 2025-08-14](http://sh.people.com.cn/n2/2025/0814/c134768-41322598.html) |
| 沪港银行历史展览馆 at Jiujiang Road and Sichuan Middle Road (四川中路299号): built 1926, "24米长的云石柜台" and banking hall as built; in 2025 "免票免预约，现场完成信息登记即可入内". *R1:* moved from the highlight into FAQ 3 ("Is the Bund free?"); the unverified "a block inland" is dropped. | Same People's Daily Shanghai page (2025) |
| Waibaidu Bridge: steel bridge over Suzhou Creek at the north end of the Bund, near where the creek joins the Huangpu. "Look south: Pudong on the left" follows from the map (Pudong is east). | [Wikipedia: The Bund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bund) |
| Public ferry 东金线: Jinling Road East pier (金陵东路渡口, 127 Zhongshan East 1st Road) to Dongchang Road pier in Pudong. Service hours conflict between sources (to 22:00 vs to about 18:30), so the story gives none. "A few minutes" is the short crossing. | [Meet in Shanghai (municipal tourism site)](https://www.meet-in-shanghai.net/cn/traffic/dock-112479/); [Sohu: 2-yuan ferry guide](https://www.sohu.com/a/989449111_120104414); [Wikipedia: The Bund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bund) |
| 1 October 2024: 462,000 visitors to the Bund waterfront; police used one-way circulation, alternating opening and phased release. | [Cailian Press, 2024](https://www.cls.cn/detail/1815574); [Shanghai Public Security Bureau (2018 measures)](https://gaj.sh.gov.cn/shga/wzXxfbGj/detail?pa=110ef360e4374a4137867bc544ffa3a0f6703d84c6d7aa82a82de2c7c038275b) |
| Sunrise: the Bund viewing platform is a recommended sunrise spot; the sun rises behind Pudong (east). Early-morning exercisers and joggers on the Bund. | [QQ News / weather forecast for Bund sunrise, 2026-09-14](https://news.qq.com/rain/a/20260914A0CS3F00); [Travel China Guide: The Bund](https://www.travelchinaguide.com/attraction/shanghai/bund.htm) |
| Metro Line 2: East Nanjing Road and Lujiazui are adjacent stations. Nanjing Road East runs inland from the Bund towards People's Square. *R1:* the first-evening route now ends at the Customs House and takes the Metro "from East Nanjing Road", since the station is inland and north of the Customs House. | [Wikipedia: The Bund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bund); Shanghai Metro Line 2 station order |
| Guide alignment: `shanghai-where-to-stay-first-trip` notes heavy weekend passenger flow on East Nanjing Road and Bund crowds; the story agrees. No Bund guide exists; reservation rules `[]`. | `content/guides/shanghai-where-to-stay-first-trip/body.en.ts` |
| *R1, pre-release check:* Xinhua (1 October 2025) confirms "The East Is Red" still plays on the hour, and reports that the Customs House closes for a major centennial restoration after its November 2025 exhibition. ## Shanghai Tower

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| 632 m, China's tallest building; Top of Shanghai Observatory on the 118th floor at 546 m with a 360° view. | [Shanghai government: Shanghai Tower](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-ScenicSpots/20240507/f3ce9cade30f4ff2a0bad6293626a232.html); [Shanghai Tower official site](https://www.shanghaitower.com/shagnhai.html) |
| The lift reaches 546 m in about 55 seconds ("仅需55秒即可直达546米高度"); the 118th-floor deck opened officially on 26 April 2017. Record claims (fastest vs second fastest) conflict, so the story makes none. | [CNR, 2017](https://www.cnr.cn/shanghai/tt/20170426/t20170426_523727308.shtml); [zh Wikipedia: 上海中心大厦](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E4%B8%8A%E6%B5%B7%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%E5%A4%A7%E5%8E%A6) |
| The facade twists 120° as it rises, which reduces wind loads by 24%. *R1:* the story keeps the visible twist ("about a third of a circle") and says only that it "eases the push of strong winds"; the 24 % figure is dropped as engineering trivia. | [Wikipedia: Shanghai Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_Tower) |
| *R1, cut:* the 1,000-tonne tuned mass damper (上海慧眼) near the top. It is not seen on an ordinary deck visit (the official route puts 125–126F in the separate SKY632 space) and the figure has no use on site. | [Shanghai Tower official site](https://www.shanghaitower.com/shagnhai.html); [zh Wikipedia: 上海中心大厦](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E4%B8%8A%E6%B5%B7%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%E5%A4%A7%E5%8E%A6) |
| "厨房三件套" nickname: Shanghai Tower 打蛋器 (egg whisk), SWFC 开瓶器 (bottle opener), Jin Mao 注射器 (syringe). | [China News Service, 2013](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/house/2013/08-06/5127927.shtml) |
| Neighbours are lower than the 546 m deck: SWFC 492 m, Jin Mao 420.5 m. Jin Mao lies north of the Shanghai Tower plot and SWFC east. | [China News Service, 2013](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/house/2013/08-06/5127927.shtml); [zh Wikipedia: 上海中心大厦](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E4%B8%8A%E6%B5%B7%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%E5%A4%A7%E5%8E%A6) |
| *R1:* Lujiazui has four towers you can go up, not three: the Jin Mao has its own 88th-floor observation deck ("在88层金茂大厦观光厅里，可以登高远眺"; about 340 m: 340.1 m per listings, the outdoor Skywalk beside it at 340.6 m), open in 2026. The story says "on the 88th floor at about 340 metres" and leaves out the Skywalk (a separate paid add-on). | [CIIE: 金茂大厦88层观光厅](https://www.ciie.org/zbh/cn/19SER/TBS/QTS/Hotspots/20190823/18009.html); [Ctrip: 金茂大厦88层观光厅](https://you.ctrip.com/sight/shanghai2/1816019.html) |
| SWFC 100th-floor deck at 474 m with transparent glass floor strips. | [China News Service, 2008](https://www.chinanews.com/sh/news/2008/08-19/1352663.shtml); [Baidu Baike: 上海环球金融中心观光厅](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E4%B8%8A%E6%B5%B7%E7%8E%AF%E7%90%83%E9%87%91%E8%9E%8D%E4%B8%AD%E5%BF%83%E8%A7%82%E5%85%89%E5%8E%85/57242693) |
| Real-name ticketing; foreign visitors use a passport. The reservation row has no verified rule, so the FAQ says "booking ahead is wise" and offers to check. | [Shanghai government, 2024-06-11](https://www.shanghai.gov.cn/nw4411/20240611/7d585934fa1e44f39a358ee54bfdfe76.html); [Shanghai Bendibao](http://sh.bendibao.com/tour/2020311/218790.shtm); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` (shanghai-tower) |
| Lujiazui (Line 2) to Shanghai Science and Technology Museum station is three stops east (Lujiazui, Dongchang Road, Century Avenue, Science and Technology Museum); Shanghai Museum East is beside it. | [Shanghai Municipal Administration of Culture and Tourism, 2024](https://whlyj.sh.gov.cn/wlyw/20241203/a4f01fa0d284419090dd69ac3f747f52.html); Shanghai Metro Line 2 station order |
| Judgments: about 1.5 hours; arrive an hour before sunset; skip on hazy days; weekdays calmer; ears may pop. | Homeground's own advice |
| *R1:* the sun sets west of Lujiazui over Puxi, "the historic center of Shanghai" across the Huangpu (not over "the old city", which suggests the old walled town around Yu Garden, to the south-west). | [Wikipedia: Puxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puxi) |
| *R1:* the 55-second ride is said once, in the highlight (and in FAQ 1 for search); the description and `why[0]` now say "less than a minute" or nothing. | Style guide: say a fact once |
| Korean hook: Lotte World Tower's Seoul Sky 118th-floor Sky Deck is at 478 m, so Shanghai Tower's 118th floor (546 m) is about 70 m higher. | [Seoul Shinmun, 2017](https://www.seoul.co.kr/news/economy/2017/03/22/20170322020014); [Lotte World Tower: Seoul Sky](https://www.lwt.co.kr/ko/entertainment/seoulsky.do) |
