## Shanghai Museum East

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Address 1952 Century Avenue, Pudong, next to the Shanghai Science and Technology Museum; fully open since 3 December 2024; nearly 120,000 m²; over 15,000 objects on show; rectangular building with a wave-like white facade. | [Shanghai Municipal Administration of Culture and Tourism, 2024-12-03](https://whlyj.sh.gov.cn/wlyw/20241203/a4f01fa0d284419090dd69ac3f747f52.html) |
| Every floor has floor-to-ceiling glass, a platform or outdoor area; chairs along corridors and at toilets; stepped seating in the atrium linking floors 1–3, with drinks and food sold beside it ("销售矿泉水、茶、果汁、咖啡……"). *R1:* `why[0]` now opens on this moment (the escalator under the skylight into the hall with stepped seats and coffee) instead of a generic "made for a long visit". | Same source |
| Roof garden 云林 on the fifth floor, "江南新古典园林" (*R1:* en/ko now say a new garden in the old style of the Yangtze delta / 장강 하류, "Suzhou's among them", not "Suzhou style"; ko avoids 강남, which Korean readers take for Seoul): east, middle and west parts; west has small bridges, flowing water and pavilions; east is an open courtyard ending in an imitation old stage. A spiral ramp links floors 3–5. Opened to the public in September 2024; regular opening not confirmed, hence "check that it is open". | Same source; [Shanghai government, 2024-09-06](https://www.shanghai.gov.cn/nw4411/20240906/d455d0e68cef4cc98a749c5946244824.html) |
| Roof garden plants and rocks: banana plants and old Taihu rocks; modern towers nearby. | [Shanghai government, 2024-09-06](https://www.shanghai.gov.cn/nw4411/20240906/d455d0e68cef4cc98a749c5946244824.html) |
| 13 permanent galleries (bronze, sculpture, jade, seals, calligraphy, painting, Shanghai painting and calligraphy, ceramics, CeraMIX, coins, Zhao Puchu calligraphy, archaeology, Jiangnan). Line 2 to Shanghai Science and Technology Museum station, Exit 8. Individuals enter by the B1 east gate. | [Shanghai government visitor guide](https://english.shanghai.gov.cn/en-MuseumsGalleries/20241205/756c96bd7dd940378b9ac056f11429e2.html) |
| *R1:* the 2F route: "按书法馆、绘画馆、海上书画馆的顺序，设计了一公里观展动线，将'书画八景'景观与中式休息座椅融入其中"; the eight scenes "汲取园林造景元素" (种蕉厅、五合廊、六法亭、双清桥 and others), hence "small garden scenes and Chinese-style benches along the way". | [The Paper, 2024-11](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_29372736); [Shanghai Observer, 2024-11-18](https://sghexport.shobserver.com/html/baijiahao/2024/11/18/1463430.html); [Shanghai Municipal Administration of Culture and Tourism, 2024-10-28](https://whlyj.sh.gov.cn/wbzx/20241028/02b820f275a94cce973e208573426ca2.html) |
| Floor plan: B1 escalator under a circular skylight ("B1 扶梯环形天窗"); 1F bronze gallery (Da Ke Ding) and sculpture (Tang white-stone bodhisattva); 2F calligraphy and painting with a designed route of about 1 km; 3F ceramics (Northern Song Ru ware dish) and coins; 4F Jiangnan crafts; 5F roof garden. "At least 7 hours" to see the whole museum properly. | [The Paper: full-museum guide, 2024-11](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_29372736) |
| Ru ware colour 天青 ("sky blue after rain", 雨过天青), used for "the blue-green of the sky after rain". | [The Paper, 2024](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_29372736) (object named); common description of Ru glaze |
| Da Ke Ding: 93.1 cm tall, *R1:* mouth 75.6 cm ("口径75.6厘米"), 201.5 kg; *R1:* "腹部饰一条两方连续的大窃曲纹（即波曲纹），环绕全器一周" (one broad wave pattern right round the belly) and "鼎足上部另饰有突出的饕餮形象三组" (a beast face at the top of each leg); 290 characters in 28 lines on the inner wall; Ke was made 膳夫 (steward of the royal table who also carried the king's orders) in honour of his grandfather and received gifts; he cast the ding to praise the king and sacrifice to his grandfather. Late Western Zhou ("nearly three thousand years"). | [zh Wikipedia: 大克鼎](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E5%A4%A7%E5%85%8B%E9%BC%8E) |
| *R1:* Pan Dayu (潘达于) became head of the household ("潘达于成为一家之主"; "主持潘氏家事的潘达于女士决定让大克鼎等再次入土埋藏") and decided the 1951 donation ("潘达于女士决定无偿捐赠大克鼎和另一件国宝大盂鼎给国家"). Korean: 판다위(潘達于) and 판(潘)씨, following the rule that people after 1911 take the Chinese reading; 대극정/대우정 stay as hanja readings of object names. | [National Museum of China](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/portals/0/web/zt/202106dayuding/) |
| Pan family: kept the Da Ke Ding and Da Yu Ding in Suzhou; buried them again after war broke out in 1937; after Suzhou fell, Japanese troops searched the Pan house several times and never found them; donated both in 1951; the Da Ke Ding has been the Shanghai Museum's signature treasure since 1952. | [National Museum of China](https://www.chnmuseum.cn/portals/0/web/zt/202106dayuding/); [The Paper, 2025](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32364797) |
| Painting and calligraphy galleries: six rotations over three years (about every six months); the first famous works (上虞帖, 苦笋帖, 高逸图) came down after about three months; 30-minute queues on the last day, up to 90 minutes reported. | [Shanghai Municipal Administration of Culture and Tourism, 2025-02-25](https://whlyj.sh.gov.cn/wbzx/20250225/c206279ec7064a4d9140e78b7b435e27.html); [QQ News, 2025-02-17](https://news.qq.com/rain/a/20250217A07DQV00) |
| Guide alignment: free walk-in for individuals with original ID at the B1 east entrance; Curio-City and Digital Gallery booked separately; capacity controls; usually closed Tuesday (story points at the guide, no weekday named); foreign-passport gate step to be confirmed; People's Square shows one ticketed exhibition from 9 July 2026 to 14 November 2027 (*R1:* `why[2]` now says "until November 2027 … a single ticketed exhibition", matching FAQ 2 and the guide). | `content/guides/shanghai-museum-east-entry-reservations/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` |
| Century Park is one stop east of the Science and Technology Museum station on Line 2. | Shanghai Metro Line 2 station order |
| zh *R1:* 佛教造像 → 佛像 (plainer); the Tang piece is a white-stone bodhisattva (白石供养菩萨像). | [The Paper, 2024-11](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_29372736) |
| Korean names: 상하이박물관 동관 (sightsI18n), 대극정(大克鼎), 대우정, 판다위(潘達于), 고대문명탐색궁, 디지털관 (reservation row names), 상하이과학기술관역, 세기공원. | `lib/sightsI18n.ts`; `lib/attractionReservations.ts` |

## Humble Administrator's Garden

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| 78 mu (52,000 m², "about five hectares"); the largest garden in Suzhou; water-centred ("以水为主"); east, middle (core) and west sections; spring azalea and summer lotus festivals. | [Suzhou Landscape and Greening Bureau](https://ylj.suzhou.gov.cn/szsylj/sjyc/201905/c1df393edc8745abb20e8a9bd5525782.shtml); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humble_Administrator%27s_Garden) |
| Founded by Wang Xianchen, a censor who left office and came home; the bureau and Suzhou Museum give 1509, Wikipedia 1513, so the story says "about five hundred years ago". | [Suzhou Landscape and Greening Bureau](https://ylj.suzhou.gov.cn/szsylj/sjyc/201905/c1df393edc8745abb20e8a9bd5525782.shtml); [Suzhou Museum](https://www.szmuseum.com/News/Details/tptgzwf); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humble_Administrator%27s_Garden) |
| Name from Pan Yue (Western Jin, 247–300), 《闲居赋》: "筑室种树……灌园鬻蔬……是亦拙者之为政也". | [Suzhou Museum](https://www.szmuseum.com/News/Details/tptgzwf); [Suzhou Landscape and Greening Bureau](https://ylj.suzhou.gov.cn/szsylj/sjyc/201905/c1df393edc8745abb20e8a9bd5525782.shtml) |
| *R1:* Wen Zhengming: the bureau says only "据传王献臣在建园之期,曾请……文徵明为其设计蓝图", so the story hedges ("is said to have helped plan", 据说, 도왔다고 전하며); that he painted the garden (album of 31 views, 1533) is secure. | [Suzhou Landscape and Greening Bureau](https://ylj.suzhou.gov.cn/szsylj/sjyc/201905/c1df393edc8745abb20e8a9bd5525782.shtml); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humble_Administrator%27s_Garden) |
| UNESCO: "Classical Chinese garden design, which seeks to recreate natural landscapes in miniature…"; inscribed 1997. | [UNESCO 813](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/813/) (page blocked to scripts; wording confirmed via search snippet) |
| From the 倚虹亭 (Leaning Rainbow Pavilion) at the east of the Middle Garden, looking across the water, the North Temple Pagoda (北寺塔, in 报恩寺) shows over bridges and a pavilion. *R1:* distance: the source says "园外1.5公里", but OSM coordinates (倚虹亭 31.3265 N, 120.6251 E; 北寺塔 31.3231 N, 120.6155 E) give about 0.9–1.0 km, so the story says "about a kilometre". *R1:* "园内水池中倒影着塔影" / "倒映池中" (hence "on a still day it shows in the pond"); "拙政园和北寺塔之间的建筑，高度均不可超过北寺塔" (hence "No building in between may rise higher than the pagoda"). The 梧竹幽居 sentence was cut to keep the highlight to three sentences; round doorways stay in `why[0]`. | [Shanghai Landscaping and City Appearance Bureau: 借景——拙政园的精妙所在](https://lhsr.sh.gov.cn/jnylwh/20200824/7cc5e5102094470b94a8f771808c1f53.html); [OSM 倚虹亭 way 508113043](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/508113043); [OSM 北寺塔 node 4570243010](https://www.openstreetmap.org/node/4570243010) | [Shanghai Landscaping and City Appearance Bureau: 借景——拙政园的精妙所在](https://lhsr.sh.gov.cn/jnylwh/20200824/7cc5e5102094470b94a8f771808c1f53.html) |
| 远香堂: main hall of the Middle Garden, a four-sided hall facing the water with long glass windows (*R1:* zh 落地长窗, plainer than 长窗; en now names Zhou Dunyi, as zh/ko did); named from Zhou Dunyi's 《爱莲说》 "香远益清"; the lotus-viewing spot in summer. | [Baidu Baike: 远香堂](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E8%BF%9C%E9%A6%99%E5%A0%82/61061794); [Suzhou Landscape and Greening Bureau](https://ylj.suzhou.gov.cn/szsylj/sjyc/201905/c1df393edc8745abb20e8a9bd5525782.shtml) |
| 与谁同坐轩: by the water, fan-shaped; "其内桌、凳、窗、门，皆为扇形" (*R1:* "table and stools", 桌子凳子, 탁자와 걸상; the unsourced "stone" table is gone; ko 사(詞) → 노랫말); name from Su Shi's 《点绛唇》 "与谁同坐，明月清风我". Built for Zhang Lüqian, said to recall his family's fan-making origins (hedged "it is said"). | [Suzhou Landscape and Greening Bureau: 与谁同坐轩](https://ylj.suzhou.gov.cn/szsylj/ylwh/201604/2bf2a231928b4ed9840d69893a7ae240.shtml); [Baidu Baike: 与谁同坐轩](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E4%B8%8E%E8%B0%81%E5%90%8C%E5%9D%90%E8%BD%A9/7273783) |
| West Garden: merchant Zhang Lüqian bought it in the late 19th century (1877 or 1879) and built the 卅六鸳鸯馆, a hall by the water, with blue and white diamond-pane glass, said to have been bought in Europe. *R1:* `why[1]` now ends "look out through it at the pond". | [Baidu Baike: 卅六鸳鸯馆](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E5%8D%85%E5%85%AD%E9%B8%B3%E9%B8%AF%E9%A6%86/10772090); [Suzhou Museum](https://www.szmuseum.com/News/Details/tptgzwf) (1879 date) |
| "7月以来，拙政园平均每天迎纳游客超2万人次" (published 26 July 2023), weekend peaks over 29,000, mostly fully booked. *R1:* the story says "In July 2023", not "the summer of 2023". | [名城苏州 (Suzhou news portal), 2023-07-26](https://news.2500sz.com/doc/2023/07/26/1019547.shtml) |
| Holiday dates can be fully booked. | [The Paper: 苏博、拙政园约满, 2023](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_24817467) |
| Azalea show in spring (2026: 20 April to 10 May; 2023 opened in late March); lotus festival from early June to mid October. The story says "about April into May" and "from about June, fullest in high summer". | [Suzhou government, 2023](http://www.suzhou.gov.cn/szsrmzf/wtly/202303/89c9351bc1e3403aa1f179591d0e444c.shtml); [Suzhou Bendibao: 2026 azalea show](https://m.suzhou.bendibao.com/xiuxian/135942.shtm); [Suzhou Bendibao: lotus festival](http://suzhou.bendibao.com/tour/20131118/40021.shtm) |
| Suzhou Museum is next door (204 Dongbei Street), by I. M. Pei; Wen Zhengming's wisteria (文藤, "四百多年") is in the 卧虬堂 courtyard of Prince Zhong's Mansion, now part of the museum; it flowers around early April and can be seen spilling over the wall from the street ("每年花盛时，从墙外经过，墙头攀援一团紫色藤蔓"; *R1:* now said in the story). | [The Paper, 2025](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_30544039); [Suzhou Museum](https://www.szmuseum.com/News/Details/tptgzwf); [Wikipedia: Suzhou Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzhou_Museum) |
| Distances: the metro station 拙政园苏博站 is next to Suzhou Museum, 400 m from the garden, with Lion Forest within 600 m; Lion Forest is about 500 m' walk from the garden. *R1:* Pingjiang Road's north end is close: Baidu Baike gives "北起东北街" (the garden's own street), en Wikipedia "to Baita Road East … near the Humble Administrator's Garden and Suzhou Museum"; OSM puts the Dongbei Street junction about 0.3–0.5 km east of the garden's south side. So "starts about ten minutes' walk away" (the 1.3 km in hualulv measures to the middle of the street). | [Shanghai Civil Affairs Bureau: Suzhou Line 6, 2024](https://mzj.sh.gov.cn/lnb-xw/20240702/efb78381e7a846e781a3fca5d7cd10d6.html); [hualulv: nearby sights](https://www.hualulv.com/11070.html); [Baidu Baike: 平江路](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E5%B9%B3%E6%B1%9F%E8%B7%AF/316435); [Wikipedia: Pingjiang Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pingjiang_Road); [OSM 东北街 way 447683182](https://www.openstreetmap.org/way/447683182) |
| Guide alignment: 2 to 2.5 hours (how-to-read guide's table); "broad water" vs Lingering's corridors vs Master-of-Nets' intimacy; real-name timed booking 1–7 days ahead on the official Suzhou Gardens channel; Suzhou Museum a separate reservation; passport steps unverified. The story names no gate, clock time or price. | `content/guides/humble-administrators-garden-tickets-entry/`; `content/guides/how-to-read-a-suzhou-garden/body.en.ts`; `lib/attractionReservations.ts` |
| Korean hook: 소쇄원 was built by 양산보 (1503–1557), who went home after his teacher 조광조 died in the 1519 기묘사화; built from the 1520s, in its present form by the late 1530s. Same early 16th century as the garden. *R1:* moved to follow the founder sentence in ko `why[1]` ("비슷한 무렵"). | [국가유산포털: 담양 소쇄원](https://www.heritage.go.kr/heri/cul/culSelectDetail.do?pageNo=1_1_1_1&VdkVgwKey=13%2C03040000%2C36); [Korean Wikipedia: 소쇄원](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EC%86%8C%EC%87%84%EC%9B%90) |
| Korean names: 졸정원 (sightsI18n, Wikidata), 북사탑 (Trip.com KR), 핑장루, 쑤저우박물관, 사자림, 유원, 망사원, 우원, 문징명, 반악, 소동파; pavilion names in hanja reading with the original once: 의홍정(倚虹亭), 원향당, 여수동좌헌 (오죽유거 no longer appears). | [Trip.com KR: 북사탑](https://kr.trip.com/moments/detail/suzhou-11-147238709/); [Triple: 쑤저우](https://triple.guide/articles/da3e80c9-bdfb-4476-9280-af30304ff979); `lib/sightsI18n.ts` |
| Not used: "one of China's four great gardens", the son who lost the garden (anecdote), and the 06:45 early opening (guide-owned, dated). *R1:* "Holiday dates sell out" softened to "can sell out" in FAQ 5 (the source reports holiday dates fully booked, not every holiday). | — |

## Lingyin Temple and Feilai Peak

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Founded in 东晋咸和元年 (326) by the Indian monk Huili (慧理), who said the peak was "中天竺国灵鹫山一小岭，不知何代飞来"; he built the temple in front of it and named the peak Feilai. 326 is exactly 1,700 years before 2026, so the story now says "about 1,700 years ago" / "大约一千七百年前" / "약 1,700년 전" (was "nearly"). Wikipedia and the card line in `lib/sightsI18n.ts` say 328; the temple's own site says 326 (open question for the owner). | [Lingyin Temple: 千年古寺 大慈灵隐](https://www.lingyinsi.com/detail_44_7946.html); [Lingyin Temple: 鹫峰从天竺飞来](https://lingyinsi.org/detail_14040.html) ("晋咸和元年（326年）"); [Wikipedia: Lingyin Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingyin_Temple) |
| Feilai Peak is limestone, "与周围群山迥异" (unlike the hills around it); "无石不奇，无树不古，无洞不幽"; caves everywhere ("洞壑遍布"); the peak is also called 灵鹫峰. | [Lingyin Temple: 鹫峰从天竺飞来](https://lingyinsi.org/detail_14040.html); [Lingyin Temple: 灵隐新志 卷六](https://www.lingyinsi.org/detail_24_902.html) |
| Caves formed by long dissolution by groundwater (长期受地下水溶蚀作用); 龙泓洞, 玉乳洞, 射旭洞, 呼猿洞 connect. | [Hangzhou Bureau of Culture and Tourism: 灵隐飞来峰，寺前洞连洞 (extract)](https://wgly.hangzhou.gov.cn/art/2023/12/8/art_1229505585_58951410.html) |
| Carvings run from the Five Dynasties (earliest dated 951) through Song to Yuan (ends 1368): roughly four centuries, starting about 1,075 years ago. why[1] now says "Over four centuries, starting about a thousand years ago" (zh "大约从一千年前起，前后四百来年"; ko "약 천 년 전부터 400년에 걸쳐") instead of a date string; faq[0] keeps "mostly between the 10th and 14th centuries" as the quotable fact. Totals vary by count (345 / 380余 / 470余), so the story says "hundreds". Peak height 168 m (not used; it is why `description` no longer calls the peak "a cliff"). | [Wikipedia (zh): 飞来峰造像](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E9%A3%9E%E6%9D%A5%E5%B3%B0%E9%80%A0%E5%83%8F); [Wikipedia: Lingyin Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingyin_Temple) |
| The laughing Maitreya in Budai form is carved on the cliff along the stream (沿溪涧一侧的石壁上), facing the path to the temple (遥对着通往灵隐的路); reclining against the rock (随意地斜靠着石壁), bare-chested, laughing, eyes "几乎笑成两个月牙形", right hand on a big cloth sack. | [Lingyin Temple: 布袋和尚与弥勒佛造像](https://lingyinsi.org/detail_1694.html) (re-read 2026-10-04) |
| The Budai group is Southern Song (1127–1279, so "about eight hundred years ago"), surrounded by the Eighteen Arhats (en "eighteen of the Buddha's disciples"), and is the largest group on the peak. | [Wikipedia (zh): 飞来峰造像](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E9%A3%9E%E6%9D%A5%E5%B3%B0%E9%80%A0%E5%83%8F); [Baidu Baike: 飞来峰造像 (extract)](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E9%A3%9E%E6%9D%A5%E5%B3%B0%E9%80%A0%E5%83%8F/5953929) |
| Longhong Cave (龙泓洞, also 观音洞/通天洞): "像前洞顶有窍如井口透天光". "Cool even in summer" comes from the line quoted on the same page ("阴森夏亦寒") and the bureau's description of the caves; it now appears once in `why` and in the practical `when`/faq[3], not in the highlight. 一线天: "循石级而上，至一线天，只见石室上面有一小圆洞漏天光". | [Lingyin Temple: 龙泓洞](https://www.lingyinsi.com/detail_46_19385.html) (re-read 2026-10-04) |
| "On a bright day" (was "on a dull day it is hard to spot"): visitor reports say the hole is tiny and not visible on overcast or rainy days. Soft source, written as a condition. | [Qunar visitor comments: 一线天](https://touch.travel.qunar.com/comment/10156635008) |
| 理公塔 stands at the mouth of Longhong Cave, Huili's burial place ("即开山慧理祖师埋骨之所"); "高二丈余，七层", rebuilt Ming Wanli 18. The story now says "the seven-storey stone pagoda" (was "the little stone pagoda"); no colour is given, because no source states one. | [Lingyin Temple: 灵隐小志](https://www.lingyinsi.org/detail_46_787.html) (re-read 2026-10-04) |
| Cold Spring (冷泉) wells up at the foot of the peak. Dong Qichang, Ming painter and calligrapher (明代著名的书画家), wrote the couplet "泉自几时冷起，峰从何处飞来" for 冷泉亭; Yu Yue later answered it. The couplet now on the pavilion is different, so the story says the lines were written "for" the pavilion, not that they hang there. en now names Dong Qichang (as zh did) and drops "couplet"; ko says "두 줄의 글귀". | [Lingyin Temple: 俞樾答董其昌冷泉亭联](https://www.lingyinsi.org/detail_5843.html) ("这副对联的题写者就是董其昌"; re-read 2026-10-04); [Lingyin Temple: 视觉灵隐 · 泉自几时冷起](https://www.lingyinsi.com/detail_12696.html); [Hangzhou.com.cn, 2023: 1200多岁的冷泉亭](https://hznews.hangzhou.com.cn/chengshi/content/2023-09/27/content_8623144_2.htm) |
| Main hall: Shakyamuni carved from 24 pieces of camphor wood, 19.6 m tall, "加莲花座共高24.8米", gilded. Story: "nearly 25 metres tall, throne included". The temple's "我国目前最大的香樟木雕坐像" is not used. | [Lingyin Temple: 千年古寺 大慈灵隐](https://www.lingyinsi.com/detail_44_7946.html) (re-read 2026-10-04) |
| Behind the main Buddha: "大殿后壁是一组大型雕塑，高20余米，全部用黏土塑成… 共有大小佛像150尊… 表现了… 善财童子南游遍参53位名师". Layout: three tiers (天、地、海); "最下层中间那尊手执净水瓶的是… 观音菩萨，脚踩鳌鱼"; "观音菩萨右侧，有一尊双手合十、身穿红肚兜的童子，他便是善财". **Revision fix:** the draft (and the review's replacement) put the boy "below" Guanyin; he stands beside her. The highlight now puts Guanyin at the bottom centre with the boy beside her, palms together, in a red bib (红肚兜 / 빨간 배두렁이). | [Lingyin Temple: 千年古寺 大慈灵隐](https://www.lingyinsi.com/detail_44_7946.html) (re-read 2026-10-04) |
| Yellow walls: the screen wall facing the temple gate is yellow with grey tiles and reads 咫尺西天 (used only as "past the yellow walls"). | [West Lake Scenic Area Administration: 佛国净土，咫尺西天悟人生 (extract)](https://westlake.hangzhou.gov.cn/art/2021/1/19/art_1639430_59030515.html) |
| Jigong (道济) "先在灵隐寺出家" (became a monk at Lingyin); the 'mad monk' (济癫) who ate meat and drank wine. "济公殿就在灵隐寺药师殿的右侧… 左右四面墙上就是十八幅壁画"; the eighteen panels run right round the hall and tell his life in order. | [Lingyin Temple: 灵隐寺十八幅最新壁画](https://lingyinsi.org/detail_808.html) (re-read 2026-10-04); [Wikipedia (zh): 济公](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E6%B5%8E%E5%85%AC) |
| The Feilai Peak scenic area includes Lingyin, Yongfu and Taoguang temples under one free, real-name, timed reservation (morning or afternoon slot), from 1 December 2025; at least a day ahead, up to 7 days; passport listed among accepted IDs; one phone number may book up to 5 visitors, so the story says every visitor needs a booking "in their own name" rather than that each must book personally. Daily caps: 35,000 weekdays, 50,000 weekends, 65,000 holidays. | [Lingyin Feilai Peak Scenic Area notice (en)](https://en.lingyinsi.org/detail_15105.html) (re-read 2026-10-04); [notice (zh)](https://www.lingyinsi.org/detail_57_19455.html) |
| On-site registration cancelled from 1 February 2026; an online waitlist replaces it. **No longer stated in the story** (the review flagged it as the most volatile detail; booking steps belong to the reservation rules). | [Lingyin Feilai Peak Scenic Area, 3 February 2026](https://en.lingyinsi.org/detail_15142.html) (re-read 2026-10-04) |
| Crowds, weekday vs weekend: for 12 May–12 June 2026, "工作日日均剩余预约量9000余位，周末日均剩余预约量5000余位" against caps of 35,000 / 50,000. Derivation: 35,000 − 9,000 ≈ 26,000 bookings on an average weekday; 50,000 − 5,000 ≈ 45,000 at weekends (both are upper bounds, since spare was "9000余" / "5000余"). Used in faq[3] as "From mid-May to mid-June 2026, about 26,000 people booked a visit on an average weekday and about 45,000 at weekends", and in `when` as "weekends draw far more visitors". **Removed:** "weekday mornings are calmest" (no source says mornings are calmer) and the cap-as-crowd reasoning; the morning slot is now advised only for pairing with West Lake. | [Hangzhou.com.cn, 17 June 2026](https://hznews.hangzhou.com.cn/chengshi/content/2026-06/17/content_9240618.htm) (read 2026-10-04) |
| Lingyin to Taoguang: "从灵隐寺走1000米山路可至韬光寺，途中经过永福寺" (story: "walk about a kilometre uphill beyond the temple… to Yongfu and Taoguang"). Taoguang sits halfway up Beigao Peak. Stone steps: visitors describe "数百级台阶" on the climb. "Through the woods" kept from the draft. **Bamboo not added:** the review proposed "through bamboo and old trees", but the only support found is a Baidu Baike search extract and a personal blog (Sohu, describing the temple garden "茂林修竹"); Baidu Baike returned 403 and Douban a bot check, so it is left out. | [Hangzhou.com.cn, 2022: 苏东坡的西湖一日游](https://hznews.hangzhou.com.cn/wghz/content/2022-03/29/content_8212424_3.htm) (mirror of the city culture bureau article; read 2026-10-04); [Lingyin Temple: 灵隐新志 卷六 古迹](https://lingyinsi.org/detail_783.html) (韬光 "位于灵隐寺右之半山"); [Sohu: 阿春游西湖 韬光寺](https://www.sohu.com/a/518300816_389279) (steps only, soft) |
| Removed claim: "the carvings set Lingyin apart from China's other big temples". Other major temples also have carved cliffs beside them, e.g. Qixia Temple in Nanjing with its Thousand Buddha Rock. Replaced with advice ("the carvings are the heart of the visit"). | [Wikipedia: Qixia Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qixia_Temple) (as cited in review) |
| Lingyin Temple and the Feilai Feng carvings lie inside the West Lake Cultural Landscape World Heritage property. Not used in the copy; the reason sameAs has no UNESCO URL is that Lingyin has no listing of its own. | [UNESCO 1334 (extract)](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/1334/) |
| Korean hook: 영축산 (靈鷲山), the mountain of Tongdosa in Yangsan, takes its name from the Indian mountain where the Buddha preached; 飞来峰 is also called 灵鹫峰. The ko text now uses the Buddhist reading throughout: 인도 영축산, 영축봉(靈鷲峰), 통도사의 영축산 (draft mixed 영취산/영취봉/영축산). | [Korean Wikipedia: 영축산](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EC%98%81%EC%B6%95%EC%82%B0); [Lingyin Temple: 灵隐新志](https://www.lingyinsi.org/detail_24_902.html) |
| Wikidata Q1070228 (en Lingyin Temple, ko 링인쓰). Korean market name 영은사·비래봉 (style guide list). | [Wikidata Q1070228](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q1070228) |
| Guide alignment: no Lingyin guide yet. The route guide puts Lingyin–Feilai Peak, a tea stop and a West Lake cruise in one Hangzhou day; the West Lake story puts Lingyin about half an hour west by car and the Longjing villages in the hills south of the temple. `pair` was reworded so it no longer copies the West Lake story's sentence. The story states no prices or clock times. | `content/guides/first-china-trip-jiangnan-6-or-beijing-11-days/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md); `lib/sightStories.ts` (`west-lake`); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` (`lingyin-feilai-peak`) |

## Liangzhu Ancient City

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| UNESCO 2019 (criteria iii, iv): an early regional state in the Late Neolithic, based on rice farming, with social differentiation and a unified belief system; earthen monuments, urban planning and a water-conservation system. Four component areas: City Site, Yaoshan, high dams and low dams/causeway. UNESCO 2019 now appears only in faq[0]. | [UNESCO 1592 (extract)](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/1592/); [Wikipedia: Archaeological ruins of Liangzhu City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_ruins_of_Liangzhu_City); guide body.en.ts |
| Liangzhu culture dates to about 5,300–4,300 years ago. The museum frames Liangzhu as one important part of a civilisation with many roots: "体现了良渚文明在中华文明“多元一体”历史发展进程中的重要地位和独特贡献". So faq[0] now says "one of the places where Chinese civilisation took shape" / "中华文明的源头之一" / "중국 문명이 처음 모습을 갖춘 곳 가운데 하나" (was "where Chinese civilisation began"), and why[1] says "at Liangzhu you can stand in a city that old" (was "is the proof on the ground"). | [Liangzhu Museum: about the museum](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/BoWuYuanJianJie/index.html); [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 良渚遗址48小时](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2025724933723.html) (re-read 2026-10-04); [Chinese government, 2024](https://english.www.gov.cn/news/202411/26/content_WS6745600ec6d0868f4e8ed6d1.html) |
| Mojiaoshan palace platform: about 670 m east–west, 450 m north–south; three palace mounds (大莫角山, 小莫角山, 乌龟山). Height: the museum's own page says "良渚先民堆筑起一座十几米的高台——莫角山", so the story now says "more than ten metres" / "十几米" / "10여 m" (the draft's "9 to 15 metres" came only from a search extract). Built on a natural hill in the west and by filling marsh in the east with silt and grass-wrapped mud, then yellow earth. | [Liangzhu Museum, 2024: 良渚古城版「历史课本」](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/20246438161.html); [Liangzhu Museum, 2024: 良渚营建](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2024304471300.html) (re-read 2026-10-04) |
| Da Mojiaoshan is the highest of the three mounds, with an open view ("大莫角山位置最高，视野开阔"; "站在大莫角山上，良渚古城内外景观一览无遗"). From the platform, "旷野之风拂过… 访客在此俯瞰大地，观赏古城三重向心的结构". | [The Paper, 2019: 良渚古城遗址公园开放预约参观](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_3955921) (re-read 2026-10-04); [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 良渚遗址48小时](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2025724933723.html) |
| Egrets (new in why[0]): a reporter crossing the park saw "稻田、荷花、果园、池塘，树林里不时还会飞出白鹭". The story hedges with "now and then" (不时 / 이따금) and does not assume the park's tour cart is running (the guide says to check internal transport). | [The Paper, 2019](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_3955921) (read 2026-10-04) |
| Three rings: palace area, inner city, outer city; villagers lived on raised man-made ground in the outer city. | [Liangzhu Museum: 第二展厅 文明圣地](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/ChangSheZhanLan/2019862044863.html) |
| Inner-city walls are earth ridges "蜿蜒在原野上"; circuit about 6,000 m; "宽约20—150米、残存最高约4米", generally about 2 m high. **Revision fix:** the draft's "20–60 m wide" was wrong; 40–60 m is the width of the stone layer under the wall ("城墙底部的垫石层约有20—40厘米厚，40—60米宽"). faq[1] now gives no width figure ("broad but mostly only about two metres high"). | [Liangzhu Museum, 2023: 良渚古城游览之城墙篇](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2023743977746.html) (read 2026-10-04); [Liangzhu Museum, 2024](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/20246438161.html) |
| Inner city about 300 ha ("围合面积约 300万平方米"). The Forbidden City is 72 ha, so "about four times" (300 / 72 ≈ 4.2). | [Liangzhu Museum, 2023: 城墙篇](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2023743977746.html); [Wikipedia (zh): 良渚古城遗址](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E8%89%AF%E6%B8%9A%E5%8F%A4%E5%9F%8E%E9%81%97%E5%9D%80); Forbidden City row in `docs/homeground-sight-stories-sources.md` |
| A water city: "城内外河道纵横"; 9 gates, 8 of them water gates and 1 land gate; houses and landings along the rivers; "先民们乘独木舟、竹筏穿梭其间". Story: "picture the fields around you laced with waterways" / "眼前这片田野，当年河道纵横". | [Liangzhu Museum, 2024: 良渚营建](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2024304471300.html) (re-read 2026-10-04); [Liangzhu Museum, 2024](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/20246438161.html) |
| Water system north-west of the city: the Tianmu hills to the west are a heavy-rain centre; "为了抵抗山洪的侵扰" the people built high dams in the valley mouths, a long embankment and low dams on the plain; "世界迄今发现最早的堤坝系统之一". Story: "Floods poured off the hills, so the builders dammed the valleys to the north-west." | [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 千秋工程](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/QiTa1/2025344760349.html) (re-read 2026-10-04); [Liangzhu Museum, 2024: 良渚营建](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2024304471300.html) |
| Grass-wrapped mud (草裹泥): silt wrapped in reeds and cogon grass (芦荻和茅草), stacked crosswise (纵横交叠). "Like sandbags" is our plain-language simile. Laohuling (one of the northern high dams, in the hills north-west of the city): "坝身高约15米，由草裹泥堆筑而成。在这里可以近距离观看五千多年前良渚古城的水坝遗址"; "如今在老虎岭水坝剖面上，我们能清晰看到一块块垄状的分层，用纵横交错的方式堆筑，这就是草裹泥". Used in `pair` (stand by the cut face of a dam about 15 m high and see the bundles). | [Liangzhu Museum, 2024: 良渚营建](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2024304471300.html); [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 良渚遗址48小时](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2025724933723.html); [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 千秋工程](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/QiTa1/2025344760349.html) (all re-read 2026-10-04) |
| South wall display: "南城墙展示点也是良渚古城遗址公园中唯一一处真实展示五千年前城墙剖面的展示点"; "南城墙的基础石与墙体的黄土都呈条垄状分布，每一条的石头与土质都略显不同，考古人员认为这应该是石头和黄土不是来自同一处山体导致的". Approach: "沿着步道前行，微风吹过，南城墙遗址在狼尾草丛中若隐若现". **New:** "城墙基础垫石呈条垄状分布… 证明大部分石头取自北面和南面的几个山谷，而每4—5立方米左右条垄块的形成，恰好反映了一组船(或竹)的运载量" (story: each strip "about what a few boats or rafts could carry"). The draft's "each a slightly different shade" was dropped: the source says the strips differ, not that their colour differs. | [Liangzhu Museum, 2024](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/20246438161.html); [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 良渚遗址48小时](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2025724933723.html); [Liangzhu Museum, 2023: 城墙篇](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2023743977746.html) (all read 2026-10-04) |
| Park scenery: rice paddies; reeds by the Zhongjiagang river ("芦苇随风摇曳"); fountain grass (狼尾草, ko 수크령) at the south wall. | [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 良渚遗址48小时](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2025724933723.html); [The Paper, 2019](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_3955921) |
| Museum: designed by David Chipperfield, opened October 2008; long volumes clad in travertine, set on an artificial lake, with courtyards between the galleries; about 4,000 m² of galleries in three halls. (Chipperfield's page is kept here as a reference but removed from `meta.sources`, per the style guide.) | [Liangzhu Museum: about the museum](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/BoWuYuanJianJie/index.html); [David Chipperfield Architects](https://davidchipperfield.com/projects/liangzhu-museum) |
| The museum has a giant sand-table model of the city ("良渚古城的规模与布局在巨型沙盘上徐徐展开"). | [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 良渚遗址48小时](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2025724933723.html) |
| The god emblem: the museum says Liangzhu people took it "以神徽像作为共同信仰的神"; Zhejiang Online hedges ("或许就是良渚人心中神的样子"). The story now says "Archaeologists see it as the god the people of Liangzhu shared" / "考古学者认为" / "고고학자들은… 봅니다" (was stated as fact). Emblem: "图案上部，是头戴“介”字形羽冠的人的形象，下部是圆圆的大眼睛、露出獠牙的猛兽" (bared fangs added); "几乎所有玉器上都会刻上或繁或简的神人兽面像". Cong: square outside, round inside. "Without metal tools" follows from the Late Neolithic date. | [Liangzhu Museum: 第三展厅 玉魂国魄](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/ChangSheZhanLan/2019662043668.html); [Zhejiang Online, 2024: 玉琮王，穿越五千年的凝视](https://zjnews.zjol.com.cn/zjnews/202411/t20241105_30623496.shtml) (read 2026-10-04); [Chinese government, 2024](https://english.www.gov.cn/news/202411/26/content_WS6745600ec6d0868f4e8ed6d1.html) |
| The King of Cong (琮王, 6.5 kg) came from Fanshan tomb 12 and is "现藏于浙江省博物馆", photographed at the Zhijiang branch (之江馆); it is "被誉为“天下第一”琮". Story (`pair` only now): "The most celebrated Liangzhu jade… is kept in Hangzhou at the Zhejiang Provincial Museum's Zhijiang branch, not at Liangzhu" / "被誉为“天下第一琮”" / "‘천하제일 옥종’으로 불리는" (replaces the draft's subjective "finest"). | [Zhejiang Online, 2024](https://zjnews.zjol.com.cn/zjnews/202411/t20241105_30623496.shtml) (read 2026-10-04); [Zhejiang Cultural Heritage Administration, 2024 (extract)](http://wwj.zj.gov.cn/art/2024/11/6/art_1639077_59062446.html) |
| Yaoshan: "瑶山遗址位于良渚古城东北方向约5公里"; the hilltop "以三种颜色不同的土营建出一个呈“回”字形状的台面". | [Liangzhu Museum, 2024: 良渚营建](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2024304471300.html) (re-read 2026-10-04) |
| Spring: rapeseed flowers at the land gate, the south and east walls, north of Mojiaoshan and elsewhere in the inner city; cherry blossom by the water gate west of the south wall ("观赏点：水城门（南城墙西）"), which the museum also calls the west water gate ("樱花湖就在其中的西水城门旁"). | [Liangzhu Museum, 2024: 春风有信](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2024184389180.html); [Liangzhu Museum, 2024](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/20246438161.html) |
| Autumn: golden rice fields and reeds in the park. | [Hangzhou Bendibao: 秋季稻田观赏地 (extract)](https://m.hz.bendibao.com/tour/94669.shtm); [The Paper, 2019](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_3955921) |
| Distance: about 25 km from central Hangzhou (other sources give about 20 km for the site and about 30 km for the park), so the story says "about 25 kilometres north-west of central Hangzhou". | [Wikipedia (zh): 良渚古城遗址](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E8%89%AF%E6%B8%9A%E5%8F%A4%E5%9F%8E%E9%81%97%E5%9D%80); [Hangzhou Bendibao (extract)](https://m.hz.bendibao.com/tour/ly74979.shtm) |
| Wikidata Q15904183 (en "Liangzhu site", ko 량주 유적), WHS id 1592. | [Wikidata Q15904183](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q15904183) |
| Guide alignment: museum first for most first-time visitors, park first for photographers, landscape lovers and repeat visitors (now also in why[2]); full day (museum morning, park afternoon) is the strongest format; a half day means the museum plus one park section; heat, heavy rain and typhoons can close the park, so keep a museum-only fallback; Yaoshan and the water system need separate checks. The story gives no booking steps. | `content/guides/liangzhu-ruins-park-and-museum-sequence/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md) |
| Reservation alignment: two venues with separate arrangements; one confirmation does not cover the other. FAQ 3 says "check each one's admission for your date". | `lib/attractionReservations.ts` (`liangzhu`) |
| `meta.sources`: the Chipperfield page was replaced by the museum's own visitor page 良渚遗址48小时 (2025), which covers the museum, the park, the south wall and Laohuling. The review proposed `https://www.lzsite.cn/` instead; it is the official heritage site but returned HTTP 550 from here (likely unreachable outside mainland China), so a page that overseas readers can open was preferred. | [Liangzhu Museum, 2025: 良渚遗址48小时](https://www.lzmuseum.cn/LiangBoXinWen/2025724933723.html); `www.lzsite.cn` (HTTP 550, 2026-10-04) |
| Korean names: 량주박물관, 량주 고성 유적 (match Trip.com KR moments and the guide's body.ko.ts); 야오산, 옥종 (guide body.ko.ts). 다모자오산(大莫角山), 반산, 라오후링, 저장성박물관 즈장관, 수크령 (狼尾草), 배두렁이 (肚兜) are standard readings or Korean words, not yet checked against Naver for market usage. | Guide body.ko.ts; [Trip.com KR: 량주 고성 유적](https://kr.trip.com/moments/detail/hangzhou-14-146371808/) (search result title) |

## Chengdu Research Base of Giant Panda Breeding

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Core area 3,570 mu (about 238 ha), 10 km from Tianfu Square; four themed zones; landscaping "recreates the giant pandas' native wild habitats"; panda "villas" scattered among hills and forest; the Giant Panda Museum opened in 2021. The Forbidden City covers 72 ha (sources doc), so "more than three times the Forbidden City". | [Panda Base: introduction (en)](https://www.panda.org.cn/en/about/introduction/); [Panda Base: 基地简介 (zh)](https://m.panda.org.cn/cn/about/introduction/); `docs/homeground-sight-stories-sources.md` (Forbidden City, 72 ha) |
| Panda Valley is the base's own Dujiangyan field centre ("内设部门"), about 50 km from Chengdu, 740–1,161 m above sea level, hence "sister site" and "hillside setting". | [Panda Base: 基地简介 (zh)](https://m.panda.org.cn/cn/about/introduction/) |
| Walk-in red panda viewing at the city base: "与园区内散养动物及进入式参观的小熊猫保持3米以上距离". The Moon Nursery (月亮产房) has a drinking-water point; the Panda Museum and the Panda Tower are named in the same notice. | [Panda Base: 游客须知](https://m.panda.org.cn/cn/service/notice/) |
| Sun Nursery (大熊猫太阳产房站) and Star Nursery (大熊猫星星产房站) are stops on the paid internal sightseeing bus, which links the South and West gates and the main areas. | [Panda Base: 观光车服务](https://m.panda.org.cn/cn/service/sightseeingbus/) |
| Giant pandas: a digestive system "more similar to that of a carnivore than an herbivore"; 70–100 lb of bamboo a day; "10 to 16 hours a day foraging and eating", the rest mostly sleeping and resting; nap between feedings; eat sitting upright "in a pose that resembles how humans sit on the floor"; pseudo-thumb "formed by an elongated and enlarged wrist bone covered with a fleshy pad of skin"; newborn "weighs 3–5 ounces", "pink, hairless, and blind". **(revision)** The story now says only "when they were born, each was pink, blind and lighter than a phone" (85–142 g; a phone is about 150–230 g), tied to the cubs on show. The gram figure and "hairless" (zh 没毛, ko 털도 없고, which en never had) were dropped in all three languages. | [Smithsonian's National Zoo: Giant panda](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/giant-panda) |
| Red pandas: ringed tails, arboreal; in human care "primarily crepuscular" and "more active in cooler weather". | [Smithsonian's National Zoo: Red panda](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/red-panda) |
| Captive pandas give birth mostly from July to September ("每年7至9月是圈养大熊猫集中产崽季节"). | [People's Daily Online Sichuan, 2024-07-19](http://sc.people.com.cn/n2/2024/0719/c345509-40917615.html) |
| The year's cubs are shown to the public around late September: 2025 cubs on 24 September 2025, on soft mats in an outdoor yard, "互相依偎、东张西望" (huddled together, looking around); 2026 cubs late September 2026, "在室外活动场，小家伙们在'奶爸'呵护下攀爬树干，瞪大眼睛好奇地四处张望" (climbing tree trunks in the keepers' care); 2022 cubs on 28 September 2022, shown in the Sun and Moon nurseries, "不久之后…陆续来到室外活动场" (official base page). **(revision)** A 21 September 2026 repost of the base's National Day notice names the Sun, Moon and Star nurseries for the 2026 cubs. Hence "huddled together on a mat, or clambering up a tree trunk with a keeper close by". "Tumbling on mats" was removed: no source. | [Guangming Daily, 2025-09-25](https://m.gmw.cn/2025-09/25/content_1304162628.htm); [CCTV, 2026-09-28](https://city.news.cctv.com/2026/09/28/VIDE9ffLvCx0dg5MVibcCF0W260928.shtml); [Panda Base: 2022 cubs, 2022-09-28](https://m.panda.org.cn/cn/culture/activities/2023-07-01/205.html); [Sohu repost of the base's 2026 holiday notice, 2026-09-21](https://www.sohu.com/a/1079148754_121118742) |
| Morning activity: "上午8点到10点左右是大熊猫最活跃的时段；上午10点到下午1点，大部分熊猫都在睡觉" (China Youth Daily report). Queue for the famous Huahua: "此处排队还需两个小时以上". The story hedges to "usually livelier in the cool of the morning" and "queues of an hour or more", and names no panda (individual animals move). **(revision)** "Around their first feed" (en FAQ 2, zh 吃第一顿的时候) was cut: no source ties peak activity to a feed. | [China Youth Daily via China News Service, 2024-02-02](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/sh/2024/02-02/10157284.shtml) |
| Guide alignment: neither venue can promise smaller crowds, closer views or more active pandas; in hot weather the city base may move pandas indoors; rain, maintenance and welfare change what is visible; at least half a day; the city base is the practical default for visitors staying in Chengdu; Panda Valley fits a Dujiangyan or Qingcheng Mountain route; separate real-name tickets; same organisation. The story says "usually", "nothing is guaranteed" and "neither can promise livelier pandas". | `content/guides/chengdu-panda-base-or-dujiangyan-panda-valley/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md) |
| Booking (FAQ 5): online, real-name, passport accepted, carry the original; on-site sales only for special cases; separate from Panda Valley. No booking window or price is given in the story. | `lib/attractionReservations.ts` ("chengdu-panda-base"); the guide |
| Heming teahouse in People's Park: "first built in 1923, has a history of 100 years" (re-checked in revision); tea and ear cleaning. **(revision)** "Serving tea since 1923" overstated the source (no claim of continuous service); the story now says "century-old" (zh 有百年历史的, ko 100년이 넘은). People's Park is beside Tianfu Square, so "about ten kilometres" back to the centre. (A "lidded cup" was dropped: no source on the page.) | [Xinhua via People's Daily Online, 2023-08-15](http://en.people.cn/n3/2023/0815/c90000-20058243.html); [Xinhua, 2023-08-14](https://english.news.cn/20230814/429b301684c24d449d8c1eb4d6906907/c.html) |
| Korean hook (ko `skip` only): Fu Bao lives at the Wolong Shenshuping base of the China Conservation and Research Center for the Giant Panda, a different institution from the Chengdu base (Munhwa Ilbo, 10 June 2026). **(revision)** Korean press search on 2026-10-04 still places her at Shenshuping (September 2026 health reports); a January 2026 Seoul Shinmun piece reports talks on a new panda loan to Korea, so this line is volatile. The guide also separates the CCRCGP's sites from the Chengdu operator. **Recheck before `ready: true`.** | [Munhwa Ilbo via Daum, 2026-06-10](https://v.daum.net/v/20260610110202719); the guide (third-venue warning) |
| Korean names: 청두 판다기지 (sights i18n, reservation rules), 판다밸리, 도강언, 청성산 (guide ko); 레서판다 (Korean Wikipedia's title for the red panda); 자이언트판다박물관 (guide ko); 워룽 선수핑 기지 and 중국자이언트판다보호연구센터 (Korean press); 학명다사(鶴鳴茶社) is the Sino-Korean reading, given with the hanja. | Guide body.ko.ts; [Korean Wikipedia](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EB%A0%88%EC%84%9C%ED%8C%90%EB%8B%A4); Munhwa Ilbo above |
| Wikidata: Q1067861 (Chengdu Research Base of Giant Panda Breeding; ko label 청두 판다 연구기지). | [Wikidata Q1067861](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q1067861) |
| **(revision)** "On a quiet morning you may hear the stalks snap": the crunch and chomp of pandas eating bamboo is audible to keepers and visitors (Smithsonian audio feature). Hedged with "may" and "on a quiet morning". | [Smithsonian's National Zoo: Melodious munches](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/news/melodious-munches-pandas-and-bamboo-shoots) |
| **(revision)** Removed in revision: "tumbling on mats"; the newborn's gram weight and "hairless"; "opened in 2021" for the museum (true, but institutional history); "serving tea since 1923"; "around their first feed"; the closing line "the moment people fly across the world for" (it echoed the Great Wall lead). ko FAQ 3 "유료 관람차" became "유료 관광 전동차", because 관람차 reads as a Ferris wheel in Korean. | Review round 1 (findings 4, 5, 19, 21) and own read-through |
| Cut: animal counts ("more than 230 pandas"), the 1987 founding with six rescued pandas, the panda-holding programme (no official source for its current status), the Monday closure trial of Villa No. 6 (volatile), and Huahua by name (she may move). | Guide source log ("do not reproduce undated animal counts"); [Panda Base: 游客须知](https://m.panda.org.cn/cn/service/notice/) |

## Sanxingdui Museum

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| New museum building: trial opening 27 July 2023; 54,400 m²; "三个沿中轴排列的覆土堆体，寓意'堆列三星'"; glass curtain wall and bronze sun-shades form "古蜀之眼", inspired by Sanxingdui's eye worship; 1,500+ objects on show; pits 3–8 yielded 17,000+ objects. **(revision)** Re-read: "两面形似眼睛的巨大玻璃幕墙，正对三星堆遗址发掘区" (two eye-shaped glass walls facing the excavation area); "堆体部分采用天然花岗石"; interior "米色的艺术混凝土墙面、古铜色的金属板、深灰色的地面". No source gives the glass a colour, so "blue" was removed; "grass-covered" became "earth-covered". The opening year and the "300+ new objects" count were cut from the story as trivia. | [Sichuan Provincial Institute of Cultural Relics and Archaeology, 2023-07-28](https://www.sckg.com/dynamics/2898.html) |
| The glass "eye" faces the site: "敦实厚重的立面透出'古蜀之眼'，向遗址区方向眺望" (design brief by the chief architect, 2022); **(revision)** the same piece gives the roof as "屋顶采用斜坡覆土形态". Hence "two huge glass walls shaped like eyes stare out towards the dig" and "three earth-covered mounds". | [Sichuan Daily (川观新闻) via Xinhua, 2022-03-29](https://www.news.cn/travel/20220329/26f1cb7f3180474a85cf3e98a3306f1a/c.html) |
| The name: Sanxingdui means "Three Star Mound", after three earthen mounds on the site (facing the crescent terrace of Moon Bay, "三星伴月"). | [Wikipedia: Sanxingdui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanxingdui); [Kyunghyang Shinmun, 2020](https://www.khan.co.kr/article/202002110600001) |
| The new museum shows the 1986 finds and 300+ objects from pits 3–8; the Sacred Tree No. 1 is "近4米高", has its own space, three tiers of three branches with flowers, fruit and crested birds, "一条神龙缘树而下", top incomplete ("尽管树顶残缺，但其高度仍然需要观众仰视"); gold-masked bronze heads; the old conservation and restoration hall still works on the original site and visitors can watch restoration. | [Xinhua, 2023-07-27](https://www.news.cn/politics/2023-07/27/c_1129770556.htm) |
| Pits 3–8 were excavated from 2020 (multi-institution research "自2020年以来"). | [Xinhua, 2023-07-27](https://www.news.cn/politics/2023-07/27/c_1129770556.htm); [Wikipedia: Sanxingdui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanxingdui) |
| Protruding-eye mask: 138 cm between the ears, 66 cm tall, "柱状的、向外突出的眼球", "面带神秘微笑"; about 3,200 years old. Eyes jut about 16 cm and ears are "形如蒲扇" (secondary reports; the story says "flare like fans" and gives no eye length). | [National Ethnic Affairs Commission (道中华), 2023-06-21](https://www.neac.gov.cn/seac/c103391/202306/1165481.shtml) |
| Bronze standing figure: 260.8 cm with base, figure 180 cm, about 180 kg; barefoot with anklets on a square base; hands "环握中空"; found "断裂为两段，仰躺在坑底部". The excavator Chen De'an thinks it held nothing (a ritual gesture); others propose a sceptre or ritual object, hence "still argued over". | [China News Service, 2023-01-14](https://www.chinanews.com.cn/dxw/2023/01-14/9935459.shtml); [Wikipedia: Sanxingdui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanxingdui) |
| Burial: many objects damaged before burial; objects placed first, "然后其上铺放象牙，最后再盖以炭渣和泥土"; fragments of one object found in different pits and rejoined; radiocarbon dates "集中在公元前1131年至1012年" (late Shang). **(revision)** The burial order holds for the six large pits (1, 2, 3, 4, 7, 8); pits 5 and 6 are a later, second batch with "没有象牙层". The dates come from pits 3, 4, 7 and 8. Hence "in most of the pits" (zh 大多数坑里, ko 대부분의 구덩이) and "radiocarbon dates from four of the pits" (zh 其中四个坑, ko 구덩이 네 곳). "Nobody knows why" became "no one knows for certain why", since theories exist. Objects were "broken (perhaps ritually disfigured), burned, and carefully buried". | [Chinese Social Sciences Net (Sun Hua), 2022](https://www.cssn.cn/kgxc/kgxc_kgxl/202209/t20220930_5545307.shtml); [Wikipedia: Sanxingdui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanxingdui) |
| **(revision)** Cancong: the Huayang Guozhi line "有蜀侯蚕丛，其目纵，始称王" is quoted beside this very mask by the National Ethnic Affairs Commission (Dao Zhong Hua, 2023-06-21), which links the mask's eyes to the "纵目" of the record. The story frames it as a feeling ("hard to stand before this face and not think of him") and does not claim the mask depicts him. "First king of Shu" renders 始称王 (the first to call himself king); zh says 最早称王的蜀王, ko 처음으로 왕이라 일컬은 촉의 군주. The ko quotes 其目縱 in traditional characters. | [National Ethnic Affairs Commission, 2023-06-21](https://www.neac.gov.cn/seac/c103391/202306/1165481.shtml) |
| **(revision)** "Wider than a doorway": 1.38 m against an ordinary door of about 0.9 m (our comparison). "Masks of beaten gold": the heads wear gold-foil masks (Wikipedia: "bronze heads with gold foil masks"; Xinhua 2023: 金面罩). | [Wikipedia: Sanxingdui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanxingdui); [Xinhua, 2023-07-27](https://www.news.cn/politics/2023-07/27/c_1129770556.htm) |
| **(revision)** Gallery lighting: no official or reference source describes the new museum's bronze galleries as dark (the 2022 renovation of the old bronze hall used "稳重的灰蓝色调"; the new building has skylights; travel blogs describe dim halls, mostly in the old building). So "looms out of the dark", "stare out of the dark", "In the dim galleries", 灯光暗下来, 从黑暗里浮出来, 昏暗的展厅 and 어두운 전시실 were all removed. | [Sichuan Online, 2022-03-21](https://sichuan.scol.com.cn/ggxw/202203/58470863.html); [Xinhua, 2022-03-29](https://www.news.cn/travel/20220329/26f1cb7f3180474a85cf3e98a3306f1a/c.html) |
| No writing has been found at Sanxingdui. | [Sixth Tone](https://www.sixthtone.com/news/1007903); [Wikipedia: Sanxingdui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanxingdui) |
| Restoration hall: opened December 2021, about 1,000 m², a glass wall divides the workrooms from the visitor side ("隔着透明玻璃墙，观众可以直接看到文物修复师的工作台", 2024); in July 2026 CCTV still describes it as a public science venue. The story hedges ("opening arrangements change"). | [Guangming Daily, 2024-10-27](https://news.gmw.cn/2024-10/27/content_37637704.htm); [CCTV via Sichuan Online, 2026-07-26](https://sichuan.scol.com.cn/ggxw/202607/83295015.html) |
| Summer is the museum's peak ("三星堆博物馆成暑期'顶流'", July 2026), hence "busiest in the summer school holidays". | [Sichuan Online, 2026-07-26](https://sichuan.scol.com.cn/ggxw/202607/83295015.html) |
| About 40 km from Chengdu, in Guanghan, by the Yazi River. | [Wikipedia: Sanxingdui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanxingdui) |
| Jinsha: in Qingyang District (west Chengdu); "thought to have emerged as the capital after the decline of Sanxingdui"; the Golden Sun Bird (太阳神鸟金饰: twelve whirling rays, four birds flying round) became the emblem of Chinese cultural heritage in 2005. It was lent to Chengdu Museum from 10 February to 8 March 2026 and is back at Jinsha. | [Wikipedia: Jinsha site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jinsha_site); [Sichuan Online, 2024-11](https://sichuan.scol.com.cn/ggxw/202411/82644541.html); [Chengdu Museum, 2026-02](https://www.cdmuseum.com/xinwen/202602/4248.html) |
| Guide alignment: real-name booking via the museum's website, official WeChat or mini-program; passport accepted for inbound visitors; carry the original; official-channel warning; two hours selective, about three hours better; train to Guanghan North is not the museum entrance, so plan the last leg and do not buy a tight return. The story keeps the "alien" theory out entirely. **(revision)** `why[2]` now points at the guide's gallery order in one clause ("see the rooms about the site and the dig before the great bronzes"), which matches the guide's "read the site and excavation story before the famous bronzes"; it does not restate the sequence. | `content/guides/sanxingdui-museum-booking-and-gallery-order/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md); `lib/attractionReservations.ts` ("sanxingdui-museum") |
| Korean names: 싼싱두이박물관 (sights i18n, reservation rules), 삼성퇴 (Korean Wikipedia title; Kyunghyang), 광한·광한북역 (guide ko), 진사유적박물관 (reservation rules). | Guide body.ko.ts; [Korean Wikipedia: 삼성퇴](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EC%82%BC%EC%84%B1%ED%87%B4) |
| Wikidata: Q7420771 (Sanxingdui Museum). The site itself is Q929072; sameAs uses the museum, which is what the page is about. | [Wikidata Q7420771](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q7420771) |
| Not used: a Korean hook from a planned 2020 Sanxingdui exhibition at the National Museum of Korea (announced; could not confirm it took place). | [Kyunghyang Shinmun, 2020](https://www.khan.co.kr/article/202002110600001) |

## Leshan Giant Buddha

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Carved out of a hillside in the 8th century, looking down on the confluence of three rivers; 71 m; listed with Mount Emei in 1996. | [UNESCO 779](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/779/) (page blocks scripted access; wording confirmed via the search index and Wikipedia's citation) |
| A seated stone Maitreya in Lingyun Hill, "坐东向西", at the meeting of the Min, Dadu and Qingyi; 71 m; about 90 years to build; 1996 World Heritage with Mount Emei. | [Xinhua, 2024-06-08](https://www.news.cn/photo/20240608/4658ec98d843495187fdeb43669037a5/c.html) |
| "世界上最大的石刻弥勒佛坐像"; "头与山齐，足踏大江，双手抚膝"; faces Leshan city across the river; a hidden drainage system behind the ears and head that has protected it. Wikipedia: "largest and tallest stone Buddha statue in the world"; "hands resting on his knees"; local saying "The mountain is a Buddha and the Buddha is a mountain". | Sichuan Department of Culture and Tourism (wlt.sc.gov.cn, 2022; via search index, host unreachable); [Wikipedia: Leshan Giant Buddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha) |
| Dimensions: head 14.7 m tall, ears 7 m, instep about 8.5 m wide. "Taller than a four-storey house" is our comparison (about 3 m a storey). | [Wikipedia: Leshan Giant Buddha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha) |
| Start date: Chinese sources give 开元初/开元元年 (713) and completion in 803; English Wikipedia gives 722 (and 712–804 in its lead). The story says "the early 700s" and "about ninety years". | [Sichuan Local Chronicles Office via The Paper](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_17790959); [Xinhua, 2024](https://www.news.cn/photo/20240608/4658ec98d843495187fdeb43669037a5/c.html); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha) |
| Haitong: boats wrecked where the three rivers meet ("三江汇流、激荡山崖……屡坏舟船"); Wei Gao's Tang record (the 大像记 stele): "时有郡吏，将求贿于禅师。师曰：自目可剜，佛财难得。吏发怒曰：尝试将来！师乃自抉其目，捧盘致之。吏因大惊，奔走祈悔。" **(revision)** The Paper's copy prints 大惋; the standard text (e.g. Sohu's transcription of the stele) reads 大惊. Hence "‘Try it, then,’ snapped the official" and "the official fled, begging forgiveness"; he died before completion (Wikipedia: completed only to the shoulders at his death); a stone statue of him was placed in an old cave "大佛头部上方左侧", named 海师洞. | [Sichuan Local Chronicles Office (方志四川) via The Paper](https://m.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_17790959); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha) |
| Drainage: 18 rows of curls, with a gutter across rows 4, 9 and 18; a connected passage behind the ears; channels in the collar and folds of the robe. **(revision)** The gutters were finished with mortar so that "远望看不出"; "look closely" was replaced by "finished so well that they vanish from a distance". | [Cover News via Tencent, 2020-08-18](https://news.qq.com/rain/a/20200818A0PUED00); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha) |
| Nine-Turn Plank Road: on the Buddha's right, "与修建佛像同时开凿", 1.45 m at its widest and 0.6 m at its narrowest, 278 steps, 70 m vertical ("相当于20层楼高"); the head platform is "'摸'大佛合影的最佳观光点". **(revision)** Since January 2020 the flow has been "从凌云栈道下行，九曲栈道上行" (down by the Lingyun Plank Road, up by the Nine-Turn). The "三四个小时" Golden Week queue was at the head platform for the old Nine-Turn descent, before 2020, and is no longer used. The same article: "患有高血压、心脏病、恐高症等疾病和年老体弱的游客请勿前往佛脚平台游览", used in `skip`. | [Chengdu Economic Daily via People's Daily Online Sichuan, 2020-01-16](http://sc.people.com.cn/n2/2020/0116/c345458-33720840.html) |
| **(revision)** The Nine-Turn is closed. The management committee shut the foot route from 7 May 2024 for "乐山大佛九曲栈道区域危岩体抢险加固工程"; the official Leshan release of 8 October 2025 says "九曲栈道现处于维护关闭状态，广大游客朋友可由凌云栈道下至佛脚正常游览"; a Chengdu travel agency's article of 9 March 2026 says it is still closed and describes the Lingyun route as "沿着崖壁开凿的Z字形窄梯，需要上下约200级台阶", with "节假日栈道排队1-2小时是常态". The Lingyun Plank Road runs along the cliff on the Buddha's left and ends at his feet (built 1983–84, about 500 m; Baidu Baike via search index). So the story names no stairway and gives no step count or width: "narrow stone stairs cut into the cliff", "which stairs go down and which come back up changes with repairs and crowds, so follow the signs", and "the queue for the stairs to his feet alone can run past an hour". | [Sina repost of the 2024-05-07 closure notice](https://finance.sina.com.cn/tech/roll/2024-05-06/doc-inauianv8999343.shtml); [乐山发布 via Sina, 2025-10-08](https://finance.sina.com.cn/jjxw/2025-10-08/doc-infteiin3338258.shtml); [Sohu, 2026-03-09](https://www.sohu.com/a/994268535_122647649) |
| **(revision)** "Nearly his full height" (FAQ 2): the Buddha is "头与山齐，足踏大江", head level with the hilltop and feet at the river, so the descent from the head platform to the feet is close to his 71 m. zh FAQ 2 quotes the phrase. | Sichuan Department of Culture and Tourism (via search index; see the superlative row); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha) |
| "抱佛脚": the Leshan government headlined the reopening of the Nine-Turn Plank Road "游客争相'抱佛脚'"; the idiom 临时抱佛脚 means last-minute cramming. **(revision)** The story now says only that "the feet are right in front of you"; "do it for real" (en) and "真抱一回" (zh) were removed, since no source says visitors may hug or touch the feet. | Leshan government news (leshan.gov.cn, via search index); the 2020 People's Daily Sichuan piece above |
| Sleeping Buddha: Wuyou Hill the head, Lingyun Hill's Qiluan and Jifeng peaks the chest, Guicheng Hill the feet, "南北直线距离约1300米"; the Giant Buddha sits within the "shoulders and chest", "佛中有佛". Wikipedia: seen "from the river", with the Buddha "as its heart". | [Sichuan Local Chronicles Office via The Paper](https://m.thepaper.cn/baijiahao_6859719); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha) |
| Lingyun Temple stands beside the Buddha ("乐山大佛……位于……凌云寺侧"). | Sichuan Department of Culture and Tourism (via search index); [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha) |
| Chengdu to Leshan is about an hour by high-speed train (the Chengdu–Mianyang–Leshan line reaches Emei in 54 minutes; Leshan comes before it). The line runs on from Leshan to Emei and Emeishan stations, the latter about 2 km from Mount Emei, hence "a short ride further down the same rail line". | [Wikipedia: Chengdu–Mianyang–Leshan intercity railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chengdu%E2%80%93Mianyang%E2%80%93Leshan_intercity_railway); [zh Wikipedia: 成绵乐城际铁路](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%88%90%E7%BB%B5%E4%B9%90%E5%9F%8E%E9%99%85%E9%93%81%E8%B7%AF) |
| Leshan food: "食在四川，味在乐山"; 跷脚牛肉, 钵钵鸡, 甜皮鸭; six food streets including 张公桥. | [Sichuan Online, 2025-06-29](https://sichuan.scol.com.cn/ggxw/202506/83074039.html) |
| **(revision)** Boats: the guide lists water level, fog, wind and storms as separate causes, so "in the summer rains, high water or fog" became "high water in the summer rains, or fog". The superlative in en FAQ 1 now matches zh and ko: "the world's largest stone-carved seated Buddha". | `content/guides/leshan-giant-buddha-land-or-boat-visit/body.en.ts` (boat, dynamic) |
| Guide alignment: land route for scale and the feet when the platform is open; boat for the whole figure; separate tickets and boarding; boats can be suspended for water level, fog and weather; choose one mode on a short day from Chengdu; assess stairs before descending; protect the return train. The story points at the guide and gives no stair direction, pier or timetable; **(revision)** it now tells readers to follow the signs for direction, as the guide does ("read the live board for stair direction"). | `content/guides/leshan-giant-buddha-land-or-boat-visit/` (body.en.ts, source-log.md); reservation rules `[]` |
| Korean names: 낙산대불 (style guide market name; Wikidata alias), 낙산·링윈산 (guide ko), 어메이산 (Korean Wikipedia title; 아미산 added as the common Sino-Korean name), 마애불 (cliff-carved Buddha, a native Korean term for 摩崖石刻造像). 우유산, 구이청산 and 링윈사 follow the guide's pinyin-based spelling of 링윈산. | Guide body.ko.ts; [Wikidata Q205131](https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q205131); [Korean Wikipedia: 어메이산](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%EC%96%B4%EB%A9%94%EC%9D%B4%EC%82%B0) |
| Cut: the 13-storey wooden pavilion (Wikipedia says the Mongols destroyed it at the end of the Yuan; Chinese sources differ), the 1,021 curls count, the 2020 flood reaching the toes, the night tour (volatile), and the carved niches along the plank road (only a 2022 survey notice). | [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leshan_Giant_Buddha); [Cover News, 2020](https://news.qq.com/rain/a/20200818A0PUED00) |

## West Lake

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Inscribed in 2011 as the "West Lake Cultural Landscape of Hangzhou". UNESCO says it influenced garden design in China, Japan and Korea. | [UNESCO 1334](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/1334/); [ICOMOS evaluation](https://whc.unesco.org/document/152404) |
| The lakeside is open and free; the shore is about 15 km. The southern parks opened free in 2002. Some sites inside charge. | [Hangzhou government, 2025](https://www.hangzhou.gov.cn/art/2025/6/24/art_812270_59114316.html); [Wikipedia (zh): 西湖](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E8%A5%BF%E6%B9%96) |
| Su Causeway was built in 1090, when Su Shi governed Hangzhou, from dredged lake mud. It is nearly 3 km long and has six arched bridges. | [West Lake Administration: 苏轼筑堤](https://westlake.hangzhou.gov.cn/art/2022/7/8/art_1639430_59037825.html); [The Paper: 苏堤六桥](https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_19443734) |
| Three Pools Mirroring the Moon has three stone pagodas in the water. The island is reached by boat. The view is on the back of the 1-yuan note (5th series). | [Wikipedia (zh): 西湖](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E8%A5%BF%E6%B9%96); [Hangzhou Bendibao](https://hz.bendibao.com/tour/201393/ly37400_5.shtm) |
| Leifeng Pagoda was built in 977 and collapsed in 1924. The new pagoda was finished in October 2002 over the old site; the ruins of the old base are shown inside. | [Wikipedia (zh): 雷峰塔](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E9%9B%B7%E5%B3%B0%E5%A1%94); [Urban China: 重建雷峰塔](http://www.urbanchina.org/content/content_7709546.html) |
| Bai Juyi (governor 822–824) built a dyke that is long gone; the Bai Causeway predates him. Su Shi built the Su Causeway in 1090. The Ten Scenes were named in the Southern Song (13th century), and Kangxi changed two of the names in 1699 ([Wikipedia (zh): 西湖十景](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E8%A5%BF%E6%B9%96%E5%8D%81%E6%99%AF); [Hangzhou News, 2024](https://hznews.hangzhou.com.cn/chengshi/content/2024-03/23/content_8705401_0.htm)). UNESCO notes its influence on Qing imperial gardens. Qianlong modelled the West Causeway of the Summer Palace on the Su Causeway. | [ICOMOS evaluation](https://whc.unesco.org/document/152404); [Travel China Guide: Summer Palace West Causeway](https://www.travelchinaguide.com/attraction/beijing/summer/western-bank.htm) |
| From 2002 Hangzhou took down the lakeside park fences and stopped charging ("还湖于民"); by October 2003 the lakeside parks were free day and night. | [Tide News](https://tidenews.com.cn/news.html?id=2505269); [Hangzhou News, 2012](https://hznews.hangzhou.com.cn/chengshi/content/2012-10/08/content_4413060_2.htm) |
| Hills enclose three sides of the lake, with the city on the fourth. | [UNESCO 1334](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/1334/) |
| Leifeng fell on 25 September 1924, partly because people had long been taking bricks as charms (see Lu Xun's essay). | [Wikipedia (zh): 雷峰塔](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E9%9B%B7%E5%B3%B0%E5%A1%94); [Hangzhou News, 2024](https://hznews.hangzhou.com.cn/wenti/content/2024-09/26/content_8792570_0.htm) |
| Longjing village lies in the hills south of Lingyin, on Longjing Road back towards the lake. | Map check (Longjing Road from Lingyin to Manjuelong) |

## Hongyadong

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| It was built in 2006 in the style of Chongqing's stilt houses, with 11 storeys on the Jialing River cliff. Floor 1 opens onto Jiabin Road by the river and floor 11 onto Cangbai Road. | [Wikipedia (zh): 洪崖洞民俗风貌区](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E6%B4%AA%E5%B4%96%E6%B4%9E%E6%B0%91%E4%BF%97%E9%A3%8E%E8%B2%8C%E5%8C%BA); [Chongqing housing commission, 2025](https://zfcxjw.cq.gov.cn/cqcjdag/csjy/202504/t20250416_14526589.html) |
| Inside are food, souvenir and bar streets. | [Wikipedia (zh): 洪崖洞民俗风貌区](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E6%B4%AA%E5%B4%96%E6%B4%9E%E6%B0%91%E4%BF%97%E9%A3%8E%E8%B2%8C%E5%8C%BA) |
| The best views are from the middle of the Qiansimen Bridge footpath, the north bank across the Jialing, and the road below, the angle compared with *Spirited Away*. | [Chongqing Bendibao](https://m.cq.bendibao.com/tour/77329.shtm); [Baidu Jingyan](https://jingyan.baidu.com/article/3d69c5517a67efb1ce02d74f.html) |
| Chaotianmen is where the Jialing meets the Yangtze; the two-river night cruises leave from there. Jiefangbei is uphill nearby. | [Wikipedia: Chaotianmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaotianmen) |
| The complex opened on 29 September 2006 and takes its name from the Hongya cave in this cliff. Where the original cave was is disputed (a 1960s witness places it below the old cableway station), so the story says only that the name comes from it. | ([Chongqing civil affairs bureau, 2024](https://mzj.cq.gov.cn/sy_218/bmdt/mzyw/202401/t20240119_12841031.html)) | [Wikipedia (zh): 洪崖洞民俗风貌区](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E6%B4%AA%E5%B4%96%E6%B4%9E%E6%B0%91%E4%BF%97%E9%A3%8E%E8%B2%8C%E5%8C%BA) |
| Walking across Qiansimen Bridge is about 800 m. | [Chongqing Bendibao](https://m.cq.bendibao.com/tour/76191.shtm) |
| The lights follow a fixed schedule (around 19:00 in winter, later in summer) that has changed more than once. | [Chongqing Bendibao](http://cq.bendibao.com/tour/2019419/76188.shtm); [NetEase](https://c.m.163.com/news/a/JV5DMQLG05565JWF.html) |
| A riverside path to Chaotianmen (2.4 km, "20-odd minutes") opened in December 2025, with the Hongyadong section then closed for works, so the story says only "about twenty minutes along the river". The Jialing is usually clearer than the Yangtze, but the colours can swap after heavy rain upstream, hence "usually". | [Xinhua Chongqing](http://www.cq.xinhuanet.com/20251208/9eec344fd4cc4aeb89694374416a65ce/c.html); [China Daily](https://tech.chinadaily.com.cn/a/202306/14/WS64894110a310dbde06d234ca.html); [Chongqing government](https://www.cq.gov.cn/zt/yyztls/lsjcx/202512/t20251205_15213290.html) |
| Chongqing is called the "8D city" (Trip.com KR: 충칭 8D 매직 시티). | [Trip.com KR](https://kr.trip.com/hotspoi/8d-magic-city-of-chongqing-150025879) |

### Forbidden City

| Fact | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Third paragraph: choose one side beyond the main route; crossing from east to west is the first thing to cut. | `forbidden-city-for-foreign-visitors` guide (side-choice callout, route-cuts "Crossing from east-side rooms to west-side rooms") |
| The Clock Gallery is in the Fengxian Hall (奉先殿) on the palace's east side and needs its own ticket. The Qing court's clocks were mostly English ("以英国产品居多"), with French and Swiss pieces and clocks made in Guangzhou, Suzhou and the palace's own workshop. The story says "most of them from England and some made in China". | [Palace Museum: 钟表馆](https://www.dpm.org.cn/pavilion/225322.html); [Baidu Baike: 钟表馆](https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E9%92%9F%E8%A1%A8%E9%A6%86/5987729) |
| Clock demonstrations no longer run regularly ("这些年没了这项表演"), so the story does not promise moving clocks or the writing-figure clock. | [Visit Beijing: 故宫钟表馆](https://www.visitbeijing.com.cn/article/4HRacJnG8Or) |
| Chuxiu Palace (储秀宫), in the Six Western Palaces where empresses and consorts lived: "慈禧入宫后曾居住储秀宫后殿，并在此生下同治皇帝"; in 1884 she moved back for her fiftieth birthday after a rebuild; "现为宫廷生活原状陈列". Baidu Baike: the furnishings are as set out for her fiftieth birthday; the museum restored that state in the 1960s. | --- | --- |

(This corrects the earlier row that attributed 마오자부 to the Trip.com KR moments post, which actually uses 모가부.) | [Trip.com KR: 마오자부](https://kr.trip.com/moments/theme/poi-maojiabu-scenic-area-81704-thorough-guides-993136/); [eastchinatrip KR](https://www.eastchinatrip.com/ko/hangzhou-west-lake-guide-for-korean-tourists/); [skyticket KR](https://ko.skyticket.com/guide/29155) ("7. 곽장(郭庄), 모가부(茅家埠)") |

The story writes 소주가(쑤저우 거리) once in `why` and 소주가 in the FAQ. | [Triple: 소주가](https://triple.guide/attractions/09754fd5-cc8d-42c2-9ed6-5bd405a331d1); [eastchinatrip KR](https://www.eastchinatrip.com/ko/summer-palace-beijing-guide-kr/) |
